# The teammate handoff: what comes off Teddy's plate, and what does not

**Status:** RESEARCH (2026-09-07; **revised 2026-09-08** against Teddy's twelve final decisions in `BRIEF.md`, the day-one split in `TEAM-HANDOFF.md`, and the `/goal` loop that is now running). Not a spec. Answers Teddy's question verbatim: "looking through the M1-M6 plan, see what I can cleanly handoff to my teammates and create a well portioned handoff and plan for them."

**Where this document sits: BEHIND `TEAM-HANDOFF.md`, not in front of it.**
`/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md` is **the day-one list Teddy already has and has already handed out** — seven rows of who-does-what (`:9-15`; `:7` is the table header and `:8` the separator), the standing no-second-writer rule (`:17`), the seven Mac-teammate items in priority order (`:47-59`), the three things Mac teammates must **not** do today (`:61`), and the end-of-run sequence (`:73-79`). **It stands. Nothing below replaces it, cancels an item, or re-assigns one out of Teddy's hands.** What this document is: **the portioned work packages behind that list** — for each item, the contract it codes against, the inputs by absolute path, the acceptance its own owner can run, its dependencies, its size and its review path — plus the packages `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` does not carry because it is a one-page list and not a work breakdown. **Every package in §4 maps to a `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` item or is labelled *additional*; the map is §0.1, and §8.1 is the same map read from the other side.** An earlier revision of this document said it "supersedes `TH` §3 items 1-7 and §5 step 5 by name"; that framing is retired. Where the evidence moves an item it **re-scopes the owner role or the acceptance of that item and says so in the map**, which is a refinement of Teddy's list, not a replacement for it.

**Present tense, corrected 2026-09-08.** `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:3` was written before the run and says the overnight loop "did not start". **It has since started and is running.** **Every live-state figure in this document is a point-in-time reading, not a fact — the loop advances while you read it.** Measured read-only on Teddy's box at **2026-09-08T04:26Z**: `~/Projects/terum-skills-app` has **29 commits** and **3 Codex merges**, `FIDELITY.md` shows **27 of 87 boards locked**, **11** run records under `.planning/codex-runs/` and **14** specs under `.planning/specs/`; the worktree root `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` exists. **Session-scratch paths are provenance, never a teammate input.** `scratchpad/…` in §1 and §9 resolves only inside this session's temp directory on Teddy's Linux box and is dead the moment the session ends. **No package in §4 names one.** Where a package needed a fact that lived only there — the 20 `run` signatures, the pinned archive, the costing rows — the fact is inlined or replaced with the clone-and-checkout recipe above.
**Five standing rules, and every package below obeys all five.** They are stated once here and repeated in each package's *Must not touch*, because a pasted section carries none of this header.

1. **Nothing in this document hands out a screen, a board, a design decision, or anything whose acceptance can only be run on Teddy's Linux box.** Where a candidate fails that test, §7 says so by name instead of dressing it up as a handoff.
2. **While the `/goal` loop runs, nobody writes to `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex`, or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17`). Goal constraint (a) fails on any dirt in the CLI checkout beyond untracked `.planning/` files, and a second writer in the design folder breaks the patch cycle. **Nothing in this document is applied to any of those four paths.**
3. **M1-M4 is the loop's, and Codex writes it.** `BRIEF.md:3` and `:18` are binding: **Codex writes every line of app code through `codex-implement` on the astra tier (`gpt-6-astra`, effort `high`); Claude writes the specs and verifies and never writes app code inline**, and Claude may write only `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `.gitignore`, `package.json`, `.planning/**`, `FIDELITY.md`, `GAPS.md` and the design-folder patches. **No package below hands a screen, the scaffold, `tools/export-design.py`, `src/fixtures/`, `src/styles/tokens.css`, the Gate A harness or the Base UI wrappers to a teammate while that runs** — §7 withholds each by name, and §7.2 carries the three things a frontend helper may pick up **after** the loop. **Scope of this rule, stated so it is not over-read:** it governs **the loop's own build** — M1-M4 inside `~/Projects/terum-skills-app` while it runs — not every future contributor. Once Teddy pushes, ordinary PRs from named owners are how SIDE2's re-home into `src/backend/tauri/**`, CI2's workflow and `HANDOFF-M5.md`, FE1's `receipt-kit/` and §7.2's three packages land, and each of those names its own review path.
4. **Printed command strings stay exactly as the boards draw them, and every change to one is a design-side call Teddy makes** (`BRIEF.md:28`, `:32-33`; `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:61`). The `share` → `connect` rename and every other verb or hint drift is **one line in `GAPS.md`**, never a teammate's PR. **No package below may open a PR that renames a printed string**, and each CLI package's *Must not touch* says so.
5. **The public `terum-skills-app` does not exist until Teddy creates it and pushes** (`BRIEF.md:43`: `git init -b main`, no remote, no push, no GitHub repo; `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:14`, `:79`). Measured while revising: the local repo has **0 remotes**. **No package below may require cloning it, opening a PR against it, or reading a file inside it before that push** — every package that would live in it is written to start outside and re-home afterwards, and each says which.

## 0. The answer in five lines

1. **Thirteen packages across five owner roles come off Teddy's plate cleanly** (CLI owner, eval owner, Node/TS backend dev, CI owner, Mac owner — §5.2's sixth lane is Teddy's own, which by definition does not come off his plate and holds no §4 package) — **plus three more (§7.2) that start only after the loop ends and he pushes** — and every one of the thirteen lives *outside* the app repo and codes against a contract already frozen on disk at `b5c0507`. **Each maps to a `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` item or is labelled additional in §0.1; `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` is the day-one list and it stands, this is the work behind it.** **How many start today depends on Teddy, and under §6's own defaults the honest count is four — once he names who takes the two unnamed lanes.** **SIDE0**, **SIDE1** and **FE1** touch no decision that *blocks their start*: every decision they touch has a default that stands — **SIDE0: 5. SIDE1: 5, 16 and 10** (10 governs whether `PROTOCOL.md` marks `copyImage` mock-only; its default is that it does). **FE1: 13, plus 4 and 5 for its merge path.** — and **CI1** joins them the moment Teddy publishes the five probe files (§2.1, minutes), its own decisions (3, 4, 10, 11) likewise standing at their defaults. **Decision 19 is deliberately not counted in any of those lists**: it is not a decision a package works *around*, it is the question of **which named individual** holds the lane — the naming caveat stated next, not a default a teammate starts under. **Three** more start the instant he sends the asks — **CLI1**, **CLI3**, **EVAL1** (decision 1's default is that *nothing* in Ryan's or Ajay's repo is authorized); **CLI2** and **CLI4** follow them in-lane and behind §7.1 item 5, and both say "Start today: no" in their own headers. **MAC1** is gated separately — on Teddy publishing the five probe files and on a named individual, not on the asks. **The staffing caveat is not decoration, but it is narrower than an earlier draft made it.** `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` is Teddy's standing answer that **Mac teammates exist and already hold the day-one list**: `TH:12` and `:13` assign M5 and M6 groundwork to "Mac teammates" on "their Macs" and "their clone", `TH:43` heads "Mac teammates: today's work, in priority order", `TH:45-59` gives them seven live items, and `TH:61` lists what "Mac teammates should NOT do today" — a not-today list is not written for people who do not exist. What is **NOT VERIFIED** is narrower and stays so: **which named individual** holds the Node/TS backend-dev and CI-owner roles (§6 decision 19), and whether the Mac is **Apple Silicon or Intel** (`TB:244`). **So, counting under `TH`'s own assignment: FE1 (Ajay) plus SIDE0, SIDE1 and CI1 start today — four — once Teddy names who on the Mac side takes the backend-dev and CI-owner work; nothing here is unowned by role, only unnamed by person.** **Eleven are accepted by a check the owner runs alone;** the two that are not are named as such: **CI2**'s Mac-owner confirmation is optional and non-gating (and CI2b waits on Teddy's J1), and **SIDE3** needs a machine running one of CI1's target OSes (§6 decisions 3 and 12).
2. **None of them is a screen, a board, the scaffold, the mock, the fixture exporter, the design system or the fidelity gate — and while the loop runs none of them could be.** Two reasons, both binding rather than cautious. **The oracle:** M1-M4's only pixel oracle is `.shots/*.png` rendered by Playwright Chromium 1243 **on Teddy's Linux box** at 0.20-0.35 % tolerance, so a teammate on any other platform literally cannot run their own acceptance and handing any of it out means both artifacts get built and one is discarded after the time is spent. **The author:** `BRIEF.md:3` and `:18` make **Codex** the writer of every line of app code, through `codex-implement` on `gpt-6-astra` at effort `high`, on Teddy's own login — Claude writes the specs, re-runs the gates and merges, and never writes app code inline. There is no seat at that keyboard to offer. **What a frontend helper may take is §7.2, and it starts after the loop ends and Teddy pushes:** Gate B baselines on the other platforms (**POST1**, which is the very thing `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:79` says the fidelity gate is waiting on), Storybook over the nine specimen sheets Gate A excludes (**POST2** — `BRIEF.md:44` cuts it "tonight", not forever), and axe per route (**POST3**).
3. **The critical path is one message, then five files, then the sidecar:** send the two §9.2 batched asks → Ryan's packaging PR (CLI1) → the sidecar (SIDE2). Only two links have unbounded latency — Ryan's review pipeline and whether a Mac exists at all — and **each is started by an act costing minutes that has not happened** (no evidence anywhere in `HO`, `TH` or `BRIEF` that either ask was sent; `TB:199` "no report establishes that the machine exists").
4. **Teddy decides four things and performs one act before anyone opens a file:** send the asks (default: nothing moves), **E vs B** for the Tauri build (default **E**), whether a Mac owner exists (default **no**), where the app's CI lives (default **a public sibling repo**) — and **publish the five Tauri probe files**, which costs minutes and gates CI1, SIDE3 and MAC1 (§2.1). Two further calls — the §9.1 capability-flag question and the sidecar runtime — are listed in §6 as still-open by the letter of the older docs, and §6 records that the first is in fact already closed by the `BRIEF`. Three calls that earlier drafts carried only in prose are now numbered: the seam path (**16**), `setup` step 8 (**17**) and the M5 build order (**18**).
5. **What stays shared, and is named as shared rather than handed over:** a typed decline on `Result`, exporting `skillTrees`, `per_case` on the receipt, the M1 config set, anything named `ui`, the three ledger rows in Ryan's `team-skill-sharing.md` (D27, D22, D21), **the one §9 sentence CLI4 needs** (Ajay drafts, Ryan approves — §9 was frozen at rev 12 on Ryan's continue and last amended by him at rev 16), and **whether anyone but Ryan may open a PR in his repo** (his answer, not a default this plan can take). Each is an approval on someone's core file or a decision in sequence between two roles, not a task — §7 and §7.1. **And one thing stays Teddy's that no §4 package covers: writing, auditing and locking `.planning/specs/2026-09-07-desktop-app-frontend.md`, which is M0's own exit criterion** (`INV:299`) — §2.1.

**Provenance of the split itself.** Three partitions of the same M0-M6 surface were written independently — by owner role, by contract, by critical path — then judged three times adversarially. The by-owner partition won all three passes; five grafts from the other two were accepted and are marked **[graft]** where they appear. ### 2.2 The one rule

**Directory ownership + a named contract file + a conformance suite the owner runs alone.** Stated as three clauses because all three have to hold:

1. **Exactly one role writes any given directory.** Ryan's repo is Ryan's; the app repo is the loop's until Teddy pushes; the sidecar is its own repo (decision 5, §6); CI owns `.github/workflows/**` in a scratch repo and nothing else; the Mac owner writes a table, not a file.
2. **Everyone codes against a named contract with a line number**, never against another package's in-progress code. §3 names all eight, says who writes each, when it freezes, and how a change is proposed.
3. **Every package's acceptance is a command its own owner can run on their own machine, today.** That is the clause that disqualifies every frontend candidate — the oracle is machine-bound — and it is also the clause that turns "the ask to Ryan" from prose into `contract/import-surface.spec.ts` (SIDE0).

A package that fails any of the three is not a handoff, it is a shared task. §7 lists those by name.

**Two things that look like contracts and are not.** `GuardAction` (`src/lib/guard.ts:10`) is deliberately **not** mirrored into the seam — `BRIEF:36`, "guard.ts is not part of the mirrored seam, do not introduce it". ### 3.1 The Backend / DTO / protocol type file and its conformance suite (K2)

**Who writes it.** The inward half — `src/backend/types.ts`, `Backend.ts`, `mock/`, `prompter.ts`, `index.ts` — is written by the `/goal` loop's S1a/S1b **under Teddy**, because it is co-designed with the mock and the screens rather than handed over a wall: the mock is where every capability-flagged affordance lives, so its shape follows from Teddy's board decisions. It is **not handoff-able** and §7 says so.

**The outward half is separable and is SIDE1.** `protocol/PROTOCOL.md` plus `protocol/conformance.spec.ts` assert on `Backend`, `Run<T>`, `Frame`, `Result<T>` and `Prompter` only — never the DOM, never `.shots/*.png` — so they run on any machine with no design folder and no fidelity oracle.

**When it freezes.** The **names** are frozen now (`BRIEF:47`, 26 members). The **file** freezes the instant Teddy pushes `terum-skills-app`. Until then SIDE1 codes against the CLI's own types at `b5c0507`, which is the point: `Prompter` and `Result<T>` are byte-identical at the pin (`HO:12`), so the mirror cannot drift.

**How a change is proposed.** A pull request against `protocol/PROTOCOL.md` with the conformance case that would fail, plus the `<path>:<line> @ b5c0507` the change mirrors. A seam name change is Teddy's (it moves the mock and the screens); a *frame* change is the protocol owner's.

### 3.2 The CLI library export surface (K1)

**Who writes it.** Ryan, in `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`, through the repo's own pipeline. **CLI1's merge and approval are wholly his**; its drafting is the Mac teammates', per `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55`. **Correction, and it reverses an earlier draft of this paragraph.** `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55` **is** the standing assignment and it supports a teammate drafting the PR verbatim: "**Draft the four packaging asks against Ryan's repo** as one PR on `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` … All four are non-breaking. **Do not merge; Ryan reviews.**" Teddy has already assigned the drafting to the Mac teammates and reserved only the merge to Ryan, so **CLI1 is drafted by a teammate per `TH:55` and reviewed and merged by Ryan** — this document does not re-assign it back. What **is** genuinely open is narrower and stays as §7.1 item 6: **whether a non-Ryan author may open a PR at all**, given `teniroo` is `admin: false` and branch protection is NOT VERIFIED. That is Ryan's answer and it rides the decision-1 ask.

**When it freezes.** When the PR merges and `npm pack --json` shows `dist/lib-entry.d.ts` inside the tarball, with the must-have assertion extended in **both** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:59` **and** `.github/workflows/release.yml:157` — verified this session: the identical list `["package.json","README.md","LICENSE","NOTICE","dist/index.js"]` is duplicated in the two files, so fixing only `ci.yml` leaves the release path able to publish a tarball with no `.d.ts` and no CI failure. **It is a five-file edit, not four.**

**How a change is proposed.** As a **failing test, not prose** — that is SIDE0's whole reason to exist. `contract/import-surface.json` names every symbol; `contract/import-surface.spec.ts` proves each one resolves. Today every **runtime** row resolves deep-import-only and every **type-only** row is absent entirely (no `declaration` in `tsconfig.build.json`); after CLI1 the runtime suite must pass against the bare specifier and the type fixture must compile. A name in neither column is the bug. SIDE0's two columns and both flips are spelled out in its own section.

### 3.3 The eval §12 contract (K5)

**Who writes it.** Ajay, in `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md`. **§12 is Ajay's and nobody else amends it; §9 is not.** Verified at the pin from the spec's own status header (`2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:3`): **rev 12 = "§9 predicates frozen (audit finding 19, applied on Ryan's continue)"** and **rev 16 = "HYG6 size cap demoted to a non-blocking warning (Ryan, 2026-09-07): inspectHygiene returns { errors, warnings }"**; revs 10, 11, 13 and 14 are Ryan's too, and the §9 API block at `:420-426` is stamped "(rev 16 — pure, per §3 and ME1)". Ajay's own revs in that header are **2-5, 8 and 15**, and the §12 card-slot reshuffles are revs **2-3** (rev 4 scrapped the verified badge tier, rev 5 made receipts append-only, rev 8 is "option 1, Ajay 2026-09-06"); revs 6, 7 and 9 carry no personal attribution — 6 and 7 are "the 2026-09-04 determinism probe". *(An earlier draft wrote "15 (and the §12 reshuffles at revs 2-6)", which mis-stated both halves; the Ryan attributions in the same sentence — revs 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 16 — check out exactly.)* **So a §9 amendment is two owners in sequence — Ajay drafts, Ryan approves and merges — which is a shared task, §7.1 item 5**, not part of EVAL1. The frontend and the CLI **cite** both sections.

**When it freezes.** §12 is already frozen at **rev 16**. Rev 17 only *adds* — the seven EVAL1 rulings are amendments and authorizations, not rewrites. `receiptSchema` itself is frozen now and every consumer below parses against it unchanged.

**How a change is proposed.** A named row in EVAL1's ruling set with the design line it serves (`build.py:<line>`) and the §12 line it tensions against. The two live tensions are recorded in EVAL1 as rulings 1 and 2 and are Ajay's to settle, not a reviewer's.

### 3.4 The `tauri.conf.json` chrome config (K6)

**Who writes it.** Teddy, as M1's owner, under the default answer E. The owner claim rests on **§2.1** (the M1-M4 single-writer row) plus **decision 2's default E**; `TB:203-213` supports only the *placement* claim — that these settings belong in M1 rather than M5 — and its own heading is "What M1 must set **regardless of who builds M5**", so it names no owner. Under B it moves to the Mac owner and MAC1 absorbs J1/J2 — **and under decision 3's default there is no Mac owner, so B without one leaves this contract unwritten** (§6 rows 2 and 3).

**When it freezes.** When M1 records the config set. Note that `TB:195` is explicit: **the acceptance tolerance for C1-C17 is unset and defining it is M1's job** — no source in the evidence set states an acceptance tolerance for a native macOS window, and the only baseline that exists is the browser-side 0-px one, which nothing converts into a gate on a `.app`. That is decision 6 in §6, not an oversight in MAC1.

**How a change is proposed.** **The Mac owner reports a delta and never edits the file.** As `TB:169` currently stands, the Mac owner is granted edit rights on `trafficLightPosition` x/y, `minWidth`/`minHeight`, `hiddenTitle` and the `data-tauri-drag-region` placement. Narrowing that to report-only is the single change that turns J1 and J4 from one shared task into two clean handoffs.

### 3.5 The CI release artefact (K7)

**Who writes it.** The CI owner, in a scratch **public** repo (CI1), then re-homed into `terum-skills-app` after Teddy pushes (CI2).

**When it freezes.** At the first green run that publishes a Release whose assets download anonymously.

**How a change is proposed.** A workflow PR plus a rerun. Two flags are **not** the CI owner's to choose and are Teddy decisions 11 and 10: `uploadUpdaterJson` (defaults **true** in v1.0.0 and would silently make GitHub Releases the app's updater endpoint) and whether a `.dmg` ships at all.

## 4. The work packages

Thirteen packages, each mapped to a `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` item or labelled additional in §0.1; three more that only start **after** the loop are in §7.2. Each section below is written to be pasted to that teammate alone. **Five rules make that true rather than asserted, and every section obeys them:** (1) **every shorthand is expanded to an absolute path plus how it travels, inside the section that uses it** — a pasted section carries none of the header block, so `INV`, `TB`, `GC`, `BRIEF`, `HO` and any bare `<report>.md` are written out where they appear; (2) **no `.review/` report is ever an input** — the design folder is not a git repo and exists as one copy (§7, re-verified: `find /home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design -name .git` returns nothing), so every fact a package took from one is inlined and the citation is provenance only; (3) **CLI-side change lists are cited into `GC`, not restated** — `GC` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-gap-costing.md`, an untracked file on Teddy's box he attaches to the ask, and a package names the **section and row id** (`GC §2 · RM-11`) so a row's change list has exactly one home and cannot drift between two documents; (4) **seam names, DTO shapes and question texts come from one place** — `BRIEF.md`'s "CLI drift at `b5c0507`" section, `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/BRIEF.md:27-37`, whose names are already built into the app repo's `src/backend/`, so **no package may propose renaming them**; (5) **the five standing rules in the header block apply to every package**, and each package's *Must not touch* repeats the two that bite hardest — the loop write rule (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17`) and the printed-string rule (`BRIEF.md:28`, `TH:61`).

**The one thing every owner must know before opening anything, restated because it is the rule most likely to be broken by a well-meaning PR.** The boards print `share`, `uninstall deploy-check`, one-line join blocks and `npx -y terum-skills@latest …` everywhere, and **at `b5c0507` several of those are wrong against the CLI**. **They stay as drawn.** Fidelity against the shots is the objective; the seam method is `connect`; the rename and every other verb or hint drift is **one line in `GAPS.md` and a design-side call Teddy makes** (`BRIEF.md:28`, `:32-33`). `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:61` says it to teammates in as many words: do not "open PRs that rename the CLI's printed strings". **No package below asks anyone to change a printed string, and none may be extended to.** The `GAPS.md` the loop actually wrote carries those lines already — 37 of them at `~/Projects/terum-skills-app/GAPS.md`, under three headings: the 18 CLI-drift lines copied verbatim from `GAPS-seed.md` (`:7-24`), the five capability-flagged gaps (`:28-32`), and fourteen other drawn affordances with no CLI counterpart (`:36-49`). It is inside the local-only app repo, so it reaches a teammate only after the push.

Every package in Ryan's repo carries the same review path and the same unknown, stated once here and repeated in each package so a pasted section stands alone: **spec delta → `/codex-spec` audit → `/codex-implement` in a worktree → `/hybrid-review`**, with `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm test` as the mechanical gate (`AGENTS.md:64-66`: "a self-reported green gate is a hypothesis the reviewer re-runs"). **Never push to `main`** — `AGENTS.md:63`, "Never `git push --no-verify`. Never push at all unless the task says so." The chain has no mild mode. **One prerequisite it carries, stated once here:** `/codex-spec` and `/codex-implement` ship with the clone (`.claude/skills/`, `.claude/commands/` at the pin) but **run on the OpenAI Codex CLI** (`codex-spec/SKILL.md:40`: "Finders | Claude | **Codex** (`gpt-5.6-sol`…)"), so they need the owner's own Codex login and quota. §2.1 reserves *Teddy's* Codex account to Teddy; it does not reserve the tool. **Where an owner has no Codex login, `/ultraspec` and `/ultrareview` are the same four dimensions on Claude alone and ship in the same clone** — `/ultraspec` as `.claude/commands/ultraspec.md` + `.claude/workflows/ultraspec.js` (**no `.claude/skills/ultraspec/` exists at the pin**; `/ultrareview` does have all three: command, `.claude/skills/ultrareview/`, `.claude/workflows/ultrareview.js`), and `codex-spec/SKILL.md:43` says which side needs an account: "Requires | nothing | `codex login status` authenticated". **`/codex-implement` is the one step in this chain with no Claude-only equivalent, and the chain says so rather than letting the substitution read as covering all three:** its Step 0 preflight is `codex login status` and it **STOPs** on `Not logged in` (`.claude/skills/codex-implement/SKILL.md:19`, `:26-28`). Without a Codex login the owner **writes the implementation himself and gates it with `/ultrareview`** — same length of trip, same gates, the code simply is not delegated. No package's acceptance below depends on the delegation. And: `teniroo` has `push: true, admin: false` on `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` — the raw API response is at `.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/refute-K1-practice.md:73`, `"permissions":{"admin":false,"maintain":false,"pull":true,"push":true,"triage":true}`, with the `workflow` token scope at `:77` and the chain at `:82` — and **whether pushing a workflow file trips branch protection or a required review is NOT VERIFIED** (`TB:259` item 19) — untestable, because `admin: false` means the protection settings cannot be read.

### CLI1 — Library packaging: make the CLI importable

**Owner role:** CLI owner (Ryan). **Size:** S — five files, no behaviour change. **Start today:** yes, once the ask is sent.

**Goal and why it matters.** Make the CLI importable as a typed library so a Node sidecar can call `run(args, io)` in-process. **Nothing else in the backend plan works until this lands**, and it gates four other packages. It is the first link on the critical path.

**Inputs to open on day one.** A checkout at the pin — `git clone https://github.com/ryanliu-terum/terum-skills && cd terum-skills && git checkout b5c0507` — then `package.json`; `tsconfig.build.json`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:54-64`; `.github/workflows/release.yml:154-160`; the 20 `run` signatures **reproduced in full below**. **That checkout is the whole input list.** `cli-libs.v2.md` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/cli-libs.v2.md`, which is **inside the design folder and in no repo** (§7; there is no `.git` anywhere under `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design`) — so it is cited below as provenance only, and every fact this package needs from it is restated inline. **Optional, and not a gate:** SIDE0's `contract/import-surface.json`, which is the mechanical acceptance list — it is produced by a *different owner* in a *different repo* and is not expected until day 2-3, so the fallback below stands in until it arrives.

**Contract.** K1 (§3.2), costed as **`GC` row RM-11** — "Packaging for in-process import: `src/lib-entry.ts` barrel, exports map, `declaration: true`, tarball CI assertion", SIMPLE · cli · approver **Ryan**, in `GC` §2 under owner file `none` (`GC` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-gap-costing.md`, an untracked file on Teddy's box that he attaches to this ask; it is in no repo). **Read RM-11's own change list there rather than a second copy of it.** The five edits, stated in full here as well so this section stands alone and so no `.review/` report has to be opened: (1) a new barrel `src/lib-entry.ts`; (2) an `exports` + `types` block in `package.json`, which today has **no `main`, no `exports`, no `types`**; (3) `"declaration": true, "declarationMap": true` in `tsconfig.build.json`, which today has exactly four `compilerOptions` keys and no `declaration`; (4) `dist/lib-entry.d.ts` added to the tarball must-have list; (5) the same addition in the second workflow. `cli-libs.v2.md:567-574` is the origin of that list, with two corrections verified at the pin:
- The tarball must-have list is duplicated in **two** workflows, so edit 4 is a **two-file** edit: `ci.yml:59` and `release.yml:157`.
- The barrel must re-export `run` from **all 20** `src/commands/*.ts` — `connect, eval, guardPush, install, invite, leave, login, ls, publish, readme, receiptCheck, search, setup, status, sync, team, uninstall, uninstallMachine, update, validate` — not the 19 `cli-libs.v2.md:571` names. `receiptCheck.ts` is new at commit `4149287`.

**Deliverables.** One PR: new `src/lib-entry.ts` (a barrel that is **not** `index.ts`); an `exports` + `types` block in `package.json`; `"declaration": true, "declarationMap": true` in `tsconfig.build.json`; `dist/lib-entry.d.ts` added to the must-have list in **both** workflows. Plus a spec delta fixing build spec §3's file tree, which is drifted — **`.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:32`** still says "`index.ts  commander wiring only`" while the wiring moved to `cli.ts:35 buildProgram`. (The file is dated **09-02**, not 09-01; `2026-09-01-…` is `team-skill-sharing.md`.) **This delta touches §3 of that file and nothing else** — CLI2's delta touches §5.2 / §6 / §6.0 of the same file, so the two are sequenced and section-disjoint; see §5.2.

**The 20 `run` signatures the barrel must re-export, reproduced here so this section is self-contained.** Re-derived at `b5c0507`; every one is `run(args, io: Prompter): Promise<Result<T>>`.

| verb | `run` at | payload |
|---|---|---|
| `login` | `src/commands/login.ts:17` | `Result<LoginResult>` |
| `setup` | `src/commands/setup.ts:75` | `Result<SetupResult>` |
| `team` (4 kinds) | `src/commands/team.ts:48-53` (overloads) | `Result<CreateResult \| JoinResult \| RemoveResult \| WorkflowUpdateResult>` |
| `leave` | `src/commands/leave.ts:19` | `Result<LeaveResult>` |
| `invite` | `src/commands/invite.ts:14` | `Result<InviteResult>` |
| `ls` | `src/commands/ls.ts:23` | `Result<LsResult>` |
| `status` | `src/commands/status.ts:26` | `Result<StatusResult>` |
| `readme` | `src/commands/readme.ts:13` | `Result<{ changed?; comment? }>` |
| `guardPush` | `src/commands/guardPush.ts:27` | `Result<GuardPushResult>` |
| `publish` | `src/commands/publish.ts:38` | `Result<PublishResult>` |
| `validate` | `src/commands/validate.ts:15` | `Result<ValidateResult>` |
| `eval` | `src/commands/eval.ts:54` | `Result<EvalResult>` |
| `receiptCheck` | `src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:18` | `Result<ReceiptCheckResult>` |
| `connect` | `src/commands/connect.ts:56-59` (overloads) | `Result<ConnectOutcome \| undefined>` |
| `install` | `src/commands/install.ts:35` | `Result<InstalledResult[]>` |
| `uninstall` (skill) | `src/commands/uninstall.ts:18` | `Result<UninstalledResult[]>` |
| `uninstallMachine` | `src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:19` | `Result<MachineUninstallResult>` |
| `sync` | `src/commands/sync.ts:35-37` (overloads) | `Result<SyncResult>` |
| `search` | `src/commands/search.ts:22` | `Result<SearchHit[]>` |
| `update` | `src/commands/update.ts:12` | **`Result<void>`** — the one verb with no payload |

`sync` is the one verb whose overload set encodes the non-interactive channel: `sync.ts:35` is `run(args: SyncArgs & { hook: true }, io: NonInteractivePrompter)`; a UI-facing sync calls the `sync.ts:36` overload with a full `Prompter`.

**Why the barrel must not be `index.ts`, stated correctly.** `src/index.ts` is the *bin*: `#!/usr/bin/env node` at `:1`, EPIPE handling at `:23-28`, and an **unconditional top-level `await buildProgram(execute, undefined, {...}).parseAsync()` at `:49`** that runs on import — it is wrapped in a `try { … } catch (error) { process.exitCode = … }` at `:48-53`, so it is *guarded against throwing*; what it has **no** guard for is being imported. There is no main-module / entry-point check anywhere in the file, so the whole CLI runs the moment anything imports it. (An earlier draft of this plan wrote "unguarded", which reads as "no try/catch" and is wrong; and an earlier draft of this plan said "it runs commander and `process.exit`, `index.ts:45,:52`". That is wrong and was corrected: `grep -rn "process\.exit(" src --include=*.ts` over non-test source returns **zero** hits; `:45` is `setExitCode: (code) => { process.exitCode = code; }` and `:52` is `process.exitCode = error instanceof CommanderError ? …`. The conclusion survives on `:49` alone.) The barrel is therefore a **third** entry point beside the bin and `cli.ts`.

**Why it is genuinely non-breaking otherwise.** Nothing under `src/` imports `src/index.ts` — its only importer is the bin itself — so a barrel adds no import cycle and no new module executes at load.

**The one hazard, verbatim** (`cli-libs.v2.md:572`): "⚠️ Adding `exports` **closes the deep-import door that is open today** — the barrel must be complete, or add explicit subpaths." Deep import (`terum-skills/dist/commands/install.js`) works today *because* there is no `exports` map (`cli-libs.v2.md:552`). This is the only one of the five edits that can break an existing consumer, and **SIDE0's suite is the mechanical guard against it**.

**Acceptance (Ryan alone), and the fallback if SIDE0 is late.** The gate Ryan runs on his own machine with nothing from anyone: `npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm test`, then `npm pack --json` with the `ci.yml:56-63` inspection block extended to require `dist/lib-entry.d.ts` **in both workflows**, then a by-hand import of the bare specifier for each of the 20 `run` names in the table above. **The five edits are fully specified by K1 and can land on that alone — SIDE0's suite is the confirmation, not the gate.** When SIDE0 has shipped, add **`vitest run contract/` green against the packed tarball via the bare specifier `terum-skills`**; that is the mechanical "done" and it replaces the hand-written negative test the source report asked for.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: **decision 1** (the ask). Also, for the *mechanical* half of its acceptance only: **SIDE0's `contract/import-surface.json` + `import-surface.spec.ts`**, produced by the **Node/TS backend developer** in the **sidecar's own repo** (decision 5) and expected day 2-3 — §5.3 and §5.4 pull SIDE0 to **Day 0** precisely so it is finished before CLI1 opens, and the by-hand fallback above covers it if it slips. Unblocks: **CLI2, SIDE2** directly, and every widened `Result` member CLI2 and CLI4 ship — those are **dead code until this lands**, because `package.json` has no `exports` and `src/index.ts` has zero `export` statements, so the app cannot read any of it (`cost-DL3.final.json`'s RM-S1 record says exactly this).

**Must not touch.** `src/lib/result.ts` (the typed decline is a separate approval, §7); `src/lib/teamRepo.ts` (`skillTrees` at `:285`, likewise); `eslint.config.js:16` (widening the prompter exemption array is a spec-level act); any verb's behaviour; `src/lib/readme.ts` and `src/lib/evals/**` (CLI3's, CLI4's). **Plus the two standing rules, which bind this package as they bind every other.** *(a)* **While the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`). Work in **your own clone** of `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`, never in Teddy's checkout: goal constraint (a) fails on any dirt there beyond untracked `.planning/` files. *(b)* **No printed string changes.** The `share` → `connect` rename and every other verb or hint drift is a `GAPS.md` line and a design-side call Teddy makes, never a PR (`BRIEF.md:28`, `TH:61`) — which is why this package is five packaging files and no behaviour change.

**Blocking Teddy decisions.** None that change the edits. Default if he says nothing about the shape: all five files, `lib-entry.ts` as the name, a complete barrel, no partial `exports`. Decision 1 gates whether it may start at all.

**Review path.** Spec delta → `/codex-spec` → `/codex-implement` in a worktree → `/hybrid-review`. Never push to `main`.

### CLI3 — Export one receipt reader (`latestReceipt`)

(`GC` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-gap-costing.md`, an untracked file on Teddy's box he attaches to this ask; it is in no repo.)

### EVAL1 — The eval-spec ruling set

**Owner role:** eval owner (Ajay). **Size:** M — spec text only, no code. **Start today:** yes **once the ask is sent** — these are amendments to a spec in Ryan's repo, and §6 decision 1's default is that nothing there is authorized.

**Goal and why it matters.** Six rulings Ajay lands alone, each phrased so the frontend and CLI4 can cite a line. **It writes exactly one file — `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md`, and within it only the sections that are Ajay's: §12, §5.3, §17 and the changelog.** Two things an earlier draft put in this package are **out**, both because they are two owners in sequence: the §9 sentence CLI4 needs (ruling 5 — §9 was frozen at rev 12 "applied on Ryan's continue" and last amended by Ryan at rev 16, `:3`) and any edit to `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md` (D27 at `:148`, D22 at `:135`). That file is **Ryan's ledger** — `**Author:** Ryan Liu` at `team-skill-sharing.md:5` — and Ryan's own §9.2 ask row already moves D21 in it. Ajay amending Ryan's ledger fails two CLEAN clauses at once (one owner; never edits a file another package edits), so **those two rows moved out of this package and into §7.1 item 4 as a named shared task, and ruling 5 moved out into §7.1 item 5 for the same reason.**

**Inputs to open on day one.** `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md` §12 (`:478-498`), §5.3 (`:195-244`), §9 (`:410-446`), §17 (`:607`+); `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md` D22 (`:135`), D27 (`:148`) — **read-only here**; they are Ryan's rows and moved to §7.1. The design values this package needs are quoted inline below, so the frozen snapshot is not an input: `lift_figure` on the library card is `build.py:615`, called at `:676` and `:1123`, and the five prose strings are at `build.py:867-878` (reproduced verbatim in **FE1**). Plus `src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50-87`. **The two shorthands used below, expanded once here so a pasted section stands alone:** **`BRIEF`** = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/BRIEF.md`, inside the design folder, which is **not a git repo and exists as one copy** — Teddy quotes the line, he does not send the file; **`INV`** = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-investigation.md`, an uncommitted file on his box that **he attaches to the ask** — it is in no repo (verified at the pin, whose `.planning/research/` holds only the three eval documents). Every fact this package takes from either is restated inline, so both are provenance you may ignore.

**Contract.** K5 (§3.3). Ajay writes §12 and §5.3; **nobody else amends §12. §9 is Ryan's** (frozen at rev 12, last amended by him at rev 16, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:3`), which is why ruling 5 is a shared task below and not one of the six.

**The six rulings this package lands — plus one it drafts and does not merge.**

1. **Lift at card level.** The design puts `lift_figure` on the library card (`build.py:615`, called at `:676` and `:1123`); §12:495-496 forbids "no lift-style decimal appears at card level". Amend, or the app hides the figure. *The defensible amendment:* a **displayed** lift shown **with** W/T/L, n and provenance ranks nothing — the rule exists because lift *ranking* is uncorrelated, not because the number is unshowable. The design already colours the figure by verdict band, not by sign.
2. **§12:480 vs D27 — does the app run evals?** **This ruling is half of a shared task, not a solo one, and §7.1 item 4 owns it.** The two specs contradict each other but they are **not both Ajay's**: §12 is his, in `2026-09-04-eval-engine.md`; **D27 is Ryan's**, in `2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md`, whose header reads `**Author:** Ryan Liu` (`:5`). §12:480 says "The UI never runs evals and never derives new statistics", reinforced by §15 ("renders the card from the receipt alone, offline"); D27 (`:148`, **PROPOSED**) says "The Evaluate button in the UI either streams the run into a panel (the local server spawns it) or opens a terminal… Leaning: stream". *Recommended shape:* D27 wins for the action, §12 keeps its data rule, and the pre-run estimate is blessed explicitly as a display of the **prior receipt's own** `efficiency` numbers × cases × k × arms. **What Ajay does alone:** write that data rule into §12 as an amendment. **What Ryan does in his own ledger:** amend D27 to point at it. Ajay's half is gated on nothing and does not wait.
3. **Report prose.** Is a UI template that **only restates numbers already in the receipt** "deriving"? The receipt has exactly one free-text field, `attribution` (`receipt.ts:57`); the design's `abstract`, `results`, `trigger_text`, `efficiency_text`, `coverage` (`build.py:867-878`) have no receipt source. *Cheapest and most likely:* permitted, with a rule that every number in the prose is traceable to a receipt field and no number may be recomputed.
4. **Authorize `per_case` in §5.3.** The shape the design consumes is `(case, candidate_score, baseline_score, (w,l,t), unscored)`. The data exists one process away: `ComparisonRow` (`execution.ts:161-174`) and `ArmSample` (`:176-191`) are serialised into `run.jsonl` by `writeRunTree` (`results.ts:18-24`) and then discarded from the receipt path. **Rule only — no CLI implementation is commissioned**, because `BRIEF:6` puts per-case tables behind a capability flag; FE1's fixtures carry it as a `.passthrough()` extra meanwhile.
5. **One §9 sentence** making the structured hygiene payload contractual. Without it **CLI4 item 1 cannot start**. It is the shortest ruling and the only one on another package's critical path — **and it is NOT Ajay's alone, so it is not one of this package's six.** §9 is Ryan's section: `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:3` records "rev 12 = §9 predicates frozen (audit finding 19, applied on Ryan's continue)" and "rev 16 = HYG6 size cap demoted to a non-blocking warning (Ryan, 2026-09-07): inspectHygiene returns { errors, warnings }", and the §9 API block at `:420-426` is stamped "(rev 16 — pure, per §3 and ME1)". **Ajay drafts the sentence; Ryan approves and merges it. That is §7.1 item 5**, and it is the same defect the D27/D22 rows were moved out for. Nothing in this package waits on it.
6. **Marketplace filter defaults.** Filtering is not literally named in §12's hard rules, but a default that hides every FAIL and NEUTRAL is an editorial stance built on receipt numbers. **This ruling *records* a call it does not make, so the call itself is now §6 decision 20 with a default that applies if Teddy says nothing: show everything — no receipt-number-derived filter is applied by default, which is the only stance §12:495-498 permits without an amendment.** Ajay writes whatever answer comes back into §12; under the default he writes the default. (Teddy's stance, recorded by Ajay.)
7. **Dashboard verdict tiles.** Counting receipts by verdict across skills is aggregation. Safe form: the tile carries the provenance it filtered on and excludes receipts from other `model` / `cc_version` pairs (§5.4:253-254).

**Bookkeeping to fold in, free.** The engine spec is **rev 16** at the pin (`:3`), not 14/15, so the drift item at **`INV:279`** — the Ajay row of the §9.2 batch, which reads "engine header says rev 14, changelog says rev 15; the integration plan pins rev 13; `--judge-escalation-model` is specified but unimplemented" — is now "reconcile the changelog against rev 16 **and record in it that the integration plan still pins rev 13**", not "the header says 14". **Scoped to the engine spec deliberately: the integration plan is a second file.** Verified at `b5c0507`: `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-integration.md:3` reads "**Status:** PLAN (rev 4, 2026-09-07)" and `:5-6` says "the engine pin moves to rev 13", while the engine spec's header reads rev 16 (`2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:3`). **Moving that pin is a write to `2026-09-04-eval-integration.md`, which is CLI4's spec-delta file and Ryan's — so Ajay records the finding in the engine changelog and Ryan moves the pin, folded into CLI4's own delta.** Ajay does not open that file. (`INV:277` is the table separator `|---|---|`; earlier drafts cited it.) `--judge-escalation-model` remains **specified but unimplemented** — re-verified: `grep -rn "judge-escalation\|judgeEscalation" src --include=*.ts` over non-test source returns zero hits, and `cli.ts:116`'s option list is `--k --triggers-only --execution-only --case --model --judge-model --working --commit --team`; absorb it into §17. **D22 (`team-skill-sharing.md:135`) still says the eval column is `"—" until phase 3` while `src/lib/readme.ts:137` fills it today — that correction is Ryan's ledger, so it moved to §7.1 item 4; Ajay supplies the finding, Ryan writes the row.** And `cli.ts:116`'s own description "never writes the team repository" is now **false** — `--commit` writes exactly one receipt (`eval.ts:196-199`).

**Acceptance (Ajay alone, today).** `/codex-spec .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md` returns no blocking findings, **and** each of the six rulings names the `build.py:<line>` it serves and the §12 / §5.3 line it amends or tensions against. Both run on his own machine against his own spec, with nothing from anyone. **One prerequisite, stated because it is not free:** `/codex-spec` ships with the clone (`.claude/skills/codex-spec/SKILL.md`, `.claude/commands/codex-spec.md` at the pin) but **runs its finders on the OpenAI Codex CLI** (`SKILL.md:40`: "Finders | Claude | **Codex** (`gpt-5.6-sol`…)"), so it needs Ajay's own Codex login and quota. §2.1 reserves *Teddy's* Codex account to Teddy; it does not reserve the tool. **If Ajay has no Codex login, the fallback gate is `/ultraspec` — the same four dimensions on Claude alone, and it ships in the same clone.** `codex-spec/SKILL.md:43` states the split in one row — "Requires | nothing | `codex login status` authenticated" — so `/ultraspec` costs no account and `/codex-spec` costs one. **Where it actually lives, corrected: `.claude/commands/ultraspec.md` plus the workflow that command invokes, `.claude/workflows/ultraspec.js`. There is no `.claude/skills/ultraspec/` directory** — `ls .claude/skills/` @ `b5c0507` returns `README.md, codex-implement, codex-spec, decision-walk, handoff, harden, hybrid-review, parallel-fix, single-fix, spec-readable, state, terum-skills, ui-ux-pro-max, ultrareview` and no `ultraspec`; an earlier revision of this line cited a skill directory that does not exist, which is the one input a teammate would have gone looking for and not found. **The same caveat applies to CLI1-CLI4's review path, which names `/codex-spec` *and* `/codex-implement` — and the two are not equally substitutable: `/codex-spec` has `/ultraspec`, `/codex-implement` has nothing.** Its Step 0 preflight is `codex login status` and it **STOPs** on `Not logged in` (`.claude/skills/codex-implement/SKILL.md:19`, and `:26-28`: "`Not logged in` → tell the user to run `! codex login` themselves … STOP"). An owner without a Codex login therefore **writes that implementation himself and gates it with `/ultrareview`**; nothing in this package's acceptance depends on the delegation, because EVAL1 is spec text and takes no `/codex-implement` trip at all.

**The deferred second check, and who triggers it.** "Every eval affordance in the app's `GAPS.md` maps to a named §12 / §5.3 / §9 line or to an authorized capability flag" is **not** runnable today: `GAPS.md` exists only at `/home/teniroo/Projects/terum-skills-app/GAPS.md`, inside the loop's local-only repo, which standing rule 5 puts out of reach until Teddy pushes and standing rule 2 write-locks meanwhile. So it is a **post-push step, triggered by Teddy's push**, not part of this package's gate. If the cross-check is wanted earlier, the seed list is at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/GAPS-seed.md` (18 `- ` lines) — inside the design folder, so it reaches Ajay only if Teddy sends it.

**The flag list this ruling set may assume, stated exactly, because "four flags" is a count and the code has five names.** `BRIEF.md:6` authorizes a capability flag for exactly four **hard, non-integral gaps** — per-machine disable, the Inbox event log **and** the `offtarget` kind, a machine registry, per-case eval tables — and **everything else is built as if real, with no flag**. As the loop actually built it, that is **five named flags**: `disablePerMachine`, `inboxEventLog`, `offtargetKind`, `machineRegistry`, `perCaseEvalTables` (plus three capability probes that are not gap flags: `windowChrome`, `openInEditor`, `clipboard`), and the mock reports every one `true`. The count differs only because `BRIEF.md:6` counts the Inbox event log and `offtarget` as one gap and the code names them separately; `GAPS.md:28-32` in the app repo carries the five as five bullets. **The only one this package touches is `perCaseEvalTables`** — which is why ruling 4 authorizes the shape and commissions no CLI implementation. **Do not invent a flag for anything else**: an eval affordance the CLI cannot serve is a `GAPS.md` line, not a new flag.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: decision 1 (the ask). Unblocks: **FE1**'s mapping table (rulings 3 and 4). **CLI4 item 1 is unblocked by §7.1 item 5, not by this package** — Ajay's draft of the §9 sentence is an input to that shared task, and Ryan's approval is what actually lands it. **§9.1 is answered** (§6 decision 0), so EVAL1 does **not** gate M0 and can land any time before M6.

**Must not touch.** **`.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md` in its entirety** — Ryan's ledger (`:5`), and specifically D21 (`:134`), which Ryan's own T1 ask amends, plus D27 (`:148`) and D22 (`:135`), all three of which are §7.1 item 4. **`.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-integration.md` in its entirety** — CLI4's spec-delta file; the rev-13-vs-rev-16 pin is a finding Ajay *records*, not an edit he makes. **§9 of his own spec** — Ryan's section (rev 12, rev 16); Ajay drafts the sentence into §7.1 item 5 rather than committing it. Any file under `src/` (CLI3 and CLI4 implement). **Plus the two standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills`** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`) — the design folder and the app repo are not just out of scope, they are **write-locked**, and this package works in your own clone of `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`; *(b)* **no printed string changes** — the five prose strings and every drawn caption stay as drawn, and a ruling that would need different copy names the copy as a `GAPS.md` line for Teddy rather than proposing an edit (`BRIEF.md:28`; `TH:61`).

Ruling 6 records that stance rather than deciding it, and under the default Ajay writes the default and does not wait.

**Review path.** `/codex-spec` on the amended spec (or `/ultraspec` if there is no Codex login — see *Acceptance*). Spec files only — no `/codex-implement` trip. **Still a PR against `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`; never push to `main`** (`AGENTS.md:63`: "Never `git push --no-verify`. Never push at all unless the task says so." — the rule is not conditioned on spec-versus-code). **Who merges it is NOT VERIFIED and this package does not assume:** §4's preamble records that branch protection and required reviews on that repo cannot be read (`teniroo` is `admin: false`), so "the eval-engine spec merges on Ajay's own approval" — which an earlier draft asserted — is retired. **Goal and why it matters.** **The ask to Ryan should not be prose. It should be a failing test that names every symbol the sidecar needs, which Ryan's PR turns green.** That test *is* the contract, it makes "is CLI1 done" a command anyone can run, and it doubles as the regression test for the hazard recorded at `cli-libs.v2.md:572` — **adding `exports` closes the deep-import door that is open today** — and a name that appears in neither column is the bug. (`cli-libs.v2.md` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/cli-libs.v2.md`, **inside the design folder, which is not a git repo and exists as one copy on Teddy's box**; the hazard is restated here in full, so the cite is provenance and you never need the file.)

The 20 `run` signatures are reproduced in full in the **CLI1** section; the two-column name list below is complete on its own.

**Deliverables.**

### SIDE1 — Wire protocol + transport-agnostic conformance suite

**Owner role:** Node/TypeScript backend developer — **unnamed — `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` assigns this work to the Mac teammates (`TH:12`, `:13`, `:43`); Teddy names the individual** (§6 decision 19, re-scoped: the open question is *which* teammate, not whether teammates exist — `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who have already received it); the package is written without a name rather than assigned to one nobody has supplied. **Size:** M. **Start today:** yes, *once Teddy names who takes it*.

**Goal and why it matters.** The outward-facing half of the seam: the document and the test suite that **both** the mock adapter and the real adapter must pass, so **M6's exit criterion — "the UI does not change" (`INV:305`) — is checkable rather than asserted**. That criterion is the whole reason the package exists, so the line is worth getting right: it is `INV:305`, not `:307` (which is a horizontal rule).

**Why it is separable when the rest of the seam is not.** It asserts only on `Backend`, `Run<T>`, `Frame`, `Result<T>` and `Prompter` — **never on the DOM and never on the `.shots`** — so it runs on any machine with no design folder and no fidelity oracle. Delivered as files outside `src/` (`protocol/PROTOCOL.md` + `protocol/conformance.spec.ts`), it collides with nothing the loop writes.

**Inputs to open on day one — one clone, and this section.** A checkout at the pin (`git clone https://github.com/ryanliu-terum/terum-skills && cd terum-skills && git checkout b5c0507`), then `src/lib/result.ts:1-6`; `src/lib/prompt.ts:11-12, :14-20, :23-26, :29-38, :40, :94, :96-106`; `src/lib/execute.ts:26-33`; `src/commands/connect.ts:202`.

**Everything else this package needs is pasted below, because none of it is in a repo.** The shorthands, expanded once here so a pasted section stands alone:
- **`INV`** = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-investigation.md`, an uncommitted file on Teddy's box that **he attaches to this ask**; it is in no repo (verified: the CLI at `b5c0507` has only the three eval documents under `.planning/research/`).   It is `Prompter` serialised, and `{"t":"result",…}` is `Result<T>` verbatim. **Record `progress` as app-invented, not mirrored** — the CLI has no progress channel at all. **And do not copy that report's next paragraph** (`:856`): it gives `Prompter` a sixth `secret` member, which is wrong at the pin — see the bullet above.

7 are mock-only in phase 1** (`skill`, `inbox`, `catalog`, `openInEditor`, `copyToClipboard`, `copyImage`, `prefs`)**; and 2 are adapter-native** (`subscribe` and `capabilities`) — **adapter-native means a real adapter implements them itself** (`subscribe` over the file watcher, `capabilities` from the adapter's own flags), **so they are neither graded against CLI data nor mock-only, and must not be counted in either bucket.** 17 + 7 + 2 = 26. **Of the other twenty, do NOT write "twenty `run(args, io)` verbs" into `PROTOCOL.md`: the count is a coincidence, not a mapping.** Only **fourteen** map 1:1 onto a `run(args, io)` verb (`status`, `search`, `install`, `uninstallSkill`, `uninstallMachine`, `connect`, `publish`, `sync`, `invite`, `team`, `setup`, `eval`, `validate`, `update`); Those are the five SIDE2 enumerates under *Five seam reads with no 1:1 CLI verb*, plus `receipts` — **six in all**. `PROTOCOL.md` must say so, because "twenty verbs" reads as coverage the seam does not have. **`copyImage` is the PNG share card's member and is governed by decision 10** (default: out of phase-1 scope, so `PROTOCOL.md` declares it and marks it mock-only, and no clipboard-image permission is requested).

}`**; **`ConnectBatch { kind: 'batch'; shared: ConnectResult[]; declined: string[]; refused: { name; reason }[] }`**; **`ConnectOutcome = ConnectResult | ConnectBatch`** (a path or option call resolves to `ConnectResult`, the bare picker call to `ConnectBatch`); **`Backend.setup(args: { target?: string; offerConnect?: boolean }): Run<SetupResult>`** (`offerConnect` defaults true, `!== false` semantics); **`Backend.uninstallMachine(args: {}): Run<MachineUninstallResult>`**; **`ValidateResult { name; findings; warnings }` on BOTH arms**, and `ValidateArgs` gains `cwd?`. **Two shapes the seam must NOT carry:** every verb's args at `b5c0507` also has `form?: InvocationForm` (`WithForm`, `1d5e3f9`) — a CLI launch detail, kept out (`BRIEF:31`, `:35`) — and `GuardAction` from `src/lib/guard.ts`, which `BRIEF:36` bars in as many words: "guard.ts is not part of the mirrored seam, do not introduce it". **Questions travel as `ask` frames answered through `Run.answer()`, never as an `io` parameter.** **The mock's failure and latency keys are `keyof Backend`, so `?__mock=error` on this verb is keyed `connect`.** **And none of this renames a drawn string:** the `#/share` route, the Share sidebar row, the `Share*` board names, `src/screens/share/` and the Invite button all stay as drawn — that page is the Members roster, not the verb. **These names are already built.** Measured in the app repo while revising: `src/backend/Backend.ts:17` is `connect(args: ConnectArgs): Run<ConnectOutcome | undefined>`, `:16` is `uninstallMachine`, `types.ts:28-29` are `ConnectBatch` / `ConnectOutcome`, `types.ts:38` is `SetupArgs { target?; offerConnect? }` and `types.ts:43` is `ValidateResult { name; findings; warnings }`. **No package may propose renaming any of them.**

**Deliverables.** `protocol/PROTOCOL.md`; `protocol/conformance.spec.ts` implementing the seven assertions below; a `scriptedPrompter` reference implementation and a stub `Backend` to run them against.

**The seven conformance assertions.**
1. Every long verb returns a `Run<T>` (`INV:127-132`) whose `frames` yields at least one frame and terminates, `done` resolves to a `Result<T>`, `cancel()` settles `done`, and `answer(id, value)` unblocks a pending `ask`.
2. An `ask` the caller did not pre-answer reaches `onUnexpected` rather than deadlocking or auto-answering. The reason, in the source's own words at **`frontend-stack.md:238`**: `onUnexpected` "makes visible, at the type level, that the UI is answering on the user's behalf, which the build spec is explicit about being dangerous (`§6.1`: 'A wizard that answered consent prompts on the user's behalf would be a bypass of §5.4')". (`:247` is the `fail?:` line of the `createMockBackend` options block at `:244-251` and says nothing about consent; earlier drafts cited it.)
3. `Prompter` has exactly five members and no `secret`.
4. **A declined verb REJECTS with an `Error` whose `message` is the decline string — it does not return a failing `Result`.** Re-verified at `b5c0507` and corrected here, because an earlier draft of this document asserted the opposite and would have shipped a conformance test that fails against the real adapter. `src/commands/connect.ts:202` is literally `throw new Error('Connect was declined.');`. **Eight of the nine decline sites throw; exactly one returns `failure()`:**

   | site | what it does | string |
   |---|---|---|
   | `src/commands/connect.ts:202` | `throw` | `'Connect was declined.'` |
   | `src/commands/connect.ts:372` | `throw` | `'Forget was declined.'` |
   | `src/commands/install.ts:145` | `throw` | `` `Consent was declined for malformed allowed-tools on ${skill.name}.` `` |
   | `src/commands/install.ts:152` | `throw` | `` `Consent was declined for ${skill.name}.` `` |
   | `src/commands/publish.ts:84` | `throw` | `'Publish was cancelled.'` |
   | `src/commands/leave.ts:38` | `throw` | `'Leave was cancelled.'` |
   | `src/commands/team.ts:121` | `throw` | `'Team removal was cancelled.'` |
   | `src/commands/team.ts:493` | `throw` | `'Invitation acceptance was declined.'` |
   | `src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:58` | **`return failure(...)`** | `'Uninstall was cancelled.'` |

   **The conversion to `{ ok: false, error }` happens nowhere inside `run()`.** It is `createExecute`'s catch at `src/lib/execute.ts:30-32` — `catch (error) { sink.stderr(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)); sink.setExitCode(1); }` — **which belongs to the bin and which a sidecar importing `run()` directly never goes through.** So: **the adapter, not the CLI, is what turns a rejection into a failing `Result`**, and this assertion is written against the adapter's boundary. Assert that a declined verb surfaces at the seam as `{ ok: false, error: 'Connect was declined.' }` **after** the adapter's own try/catch (SIDE2 owns that mapping and mirrors `execute.ts:30-32`), and assert separately that the underlying `run()` rejects. `cli-commands.v2.md:737`'s `'Share was declined.'` matches nothing at the pin.
5. `subscribe` is either a working push channel **or** an explicit no-op: **a no-op `subscribe` returns a callable `unsubscribe` and never throws, and every read remains correct with no push ever arriving.** The rule it encodes, from `gap-4.md:336`: "`subscribe` is **optional on purpose**. A future HTTP adapter for a hosted tier may have no such" channel. *Stated this way on purpose:* "the app renders correctly with the no-op" is an app-level fact, and this suite owns no app.
6. Every read parses against the zod schemas at the fixture boundary.
7. **`capabilities()` resolves before any other `Backend` member is called, and the object it returns is the only source of feature branching named in `PROTOCOL.md`** (`BRIEF:47`). *Stated this way on purpose:* the original phrasing — "answered before first paint and no component branches on `isTauri()`" — is checkable only against `src/components/**`, which the loop owns and this package's must-not-touch forbids. **The `isTauri()` half ships instead as a grep-based lint rule handed to the loop as an M1 note** (`BRIEF:47`'s own "a vitest test greps the tree and an eslint `no-restricted-imports` rule enforces it"), not as a SIDE1 test. *(That boundary is already enforced in the app repo: `src/backend/tauri/` exists, nothing outside `src/backend/` imports `@tauri-apps/*`, and a vitest grep plus an eslint `no-restricted-imports` rule holds it — so decision 16's default is realised, not merely recommended.)*

   **The flag list `PROTOCOL.md` declares, and it is closed.** `capabilities()` is **not** a per-affordance flag map. `BRIEF.md:6` authorizes a capability flag for exactly four hard, non-integral gaps — **per-machine disable, the Inbox event log and the `offtarget` kind, a machine registry, per-case eval tables** — and **everything else is built as if real: the mock says yes, no flag**, with every other drawn affordance the CLI lacks recorded as one line in `GAPS.md`. As the loop built it that is **five named booleans**: `disablePerMachine`, `inboxEventLog`, `offtargetKind`, `machineRegistry`, `perCaseEvalTables` (the count differs only because `BRIEF.md:6` counts the Inbox event log and `offtarget` as one gap), plus three capability *probes* that are not gap flags — `windowChrome`, `openInEditor`, `clipboard` — and the mock reports all of them `true`. **`PROTOCOL.md` declares exactly that set and no more**, and the conformance case asserts the object's keys are that set: a sixth gap flag appearing later is a change to `BRIEF.md:6`, which is a FINAL decision and Teddy's, not a protocol revision. **A member with no CLI source is mock-only with a `GAPS.md` line — never a new flag.**

**Plus the six refresh scenarios as named fixture states of the stub `Backend`** (`gap-4.md:687-696`) — each one a named state that produces the exact `Result` / `Frame` sequence gap-4 specifies, asserted at the seam: `hookplaced` (`subscribe` → `{sources:[{kind:'config'},{kind:'placed'}], origin:'local-write'}` after 4 s), `synced` (`{sources:[{kind:'stamp',team:'terum'}], origin:'sync'}`), `stale` (`status()` returns `stale:true` for every team), `cloneabsent` (`status()` returns `clone:{state:'absent'}`), `installhalf` (`install()` → `{ok:false, error:'…push was rejected…'}` **and** the next `library()` includes the card — proves the no-rollback rule), `synclocked` (`{ok:true, value:{placed:0, changed:false, notices:['Skipping terum: another terum-skills sync holds its session lock …'], deferred:[]}}` — proves "Sync now renders the `SyncResult`, never a green check"). **Binding each state to a `?__mock=` route is M1's job, not SIDE1's** — gap-4 itself says the six "map to a Playwright route" (`:695-696`), and routes exist only in the app.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** `vitest run protocol/` green against the stub, on any machine, with no browser and no design folder. **What it deliberately does not cover, so nobody reads it as coverage it is not:** first paint, component-level branching, and the `?__mock=` routes — three app-level facts handed to M1 as notes above.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: nothing — it codes against the CLI's own types at the pin, which is the point. `types.ts` is **named** now (`BRIEF:47`) and **materialised** when the loop's S1a lands; declare K2 frozen the moment Teddy pushes `terum-skills-app`. Unblocks: **SIDE2**, and M6's grading.

**Must not touch.** Anything under `src/` in the app repo; the CLI repo. **Plus the standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`) — which is why this package's home is the sidecar's own repo and its acceptance runs on the owner's machine; *(b)* **the public `terum-skills-app` does not exist until Teddy pushes** (`TH:14`, `:61`), so nothing here clones it, and `types.ts` is mirrored from the CLI's own types at the pin rather than read out of the app repo; *(c)* **no printed string changes** — `PROTOCOL.md` describes frames and members, never a user-facing string, and the `share`/`connect` split is exactly the boundary: the **method** is `connect`, the **route, sidebar row, board names and Invite button** stay `Share` as drawn (`BRIEF.md:28`, `:35`; `TH:61`).

**Blocking Teddy decisions, with their defaults.** **5 — sidecar repo placement; default: its own repo**, so `protocol/` sits there. **16 — the seam path, `src/backend/tauri/` vs `src/api/tauri/`; default `src/backend/tauri/`**, which is what `PROTOCOL.md`'s directory names use. **10 — is the PNG share card in phase-1 scope; default: no**, so `PROTOCOL.md` declares `copyImage` as a member (it is one of `BRIEF:47`'s 26) and marks it **mock-only in phase 1**, with no clipboard-image permission and no conformance case against real data. **19 — is this role staffed at all; default: no**, in which case the package is unowned. Every one of the four has a default that stands, so nothing here waits on Teddy.

**Review path.** Its own repo.
