### SIDE2 — The sidecar: in-process import, stdio only

**Owner role:** Node/TypeScript backend developer — **unnamed — `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` assigns this work to the Mac teammates (`TH:12`, `:13`, `:43`); Teddy names the individual** (§6 decision 19, re-scoped: the open question is *which* teammate, not whether teammates exist — `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who have already received it). **Until he does, §5.1 link 3 — the critical path — has no named owner**, which is the loudest consequence of leaving decision 19 unanswered anywhere in this plan. **Size:** L — the only L package on the critical path. **Start today:** no; it can **start against source types immediately** and **ship** only after CLI1.

**Goal and why it matters.** Drive all 20 verbs in-process through an injected `Prompter`, so the desktop app talks to the real CLI without a port, a second binary, or a spec-level change to Ryan's repo. This is M6.

**Inputs to open on day one.** A checkout at the pin — `git clone https://github.com/ryanliu-terum/terum-skills && cd terum-skills && git checkout b5c0507` — then SIDE0's `contract/import-surface.json` (both columns) and SIDE1's `protocol/PROTOCOL.md`, both in the sidecar's own repo. **The 20 `run` signatures with `file:line` are reproduced in full in the CLI1 section of this document**, so nothing here points at a session-scratch report. Plus `src/lib/config.ts:11-20, :37`; `src/lib/runner.ts:8-10`; `src/lib/auth.ts:42-44`; `src/lib/execute.ts:26-33`; `src/commands/install.ts:90, :92, :123, :238`; `src/commands/ls.ts:20, :59`; `src/lib/hook.ts:149`; `src/commands/sync.ts:106`. And **`INV:99-141`** (the `Backend` / `Run<T>` / frame block), where **`INV` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-investigation.md`** — an uncommitted file on Teddy's box that **he attaches to this ask**; it is in no repo, verified against the pin. Same for **`BRIEF` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/BRIEF.md`**, inside the design folder, which is not a git repo and exists as one copy — every `BRIEF` fact this package needs is quoted inline below. **One of them is the whole seam surface and is worth asking Teddy for by name: `BRIEF.md`'s "CLI drift at `b5c0507`" section, `:27-37`.** It is the authority for every verb name, DTO shape and question text this adapter mirrors — `Backend.connect` / `ConnectArgs` / `ConnectResult` / `ConnectBatch` / `ConnectOutcome`, `Backend.setup({target?, offerConnect?})`, `Backend.uninstallMachine({})`, `ValidateResult { name; findings; warnings }` on both arms, `ValidateArgs.cwd?`, the five-member `Prompter` with no `secret()`, and the two things the seam must **not** carry (`form?: InvocationForm`, `GuardAction`). **Those names are already built in the app repo's `src/backend/`** — `Backend.ts:16-17`, `types.ts:28-29`, `:38`, `:43` — so this package mirrors them and never proposes a rename. **SIDE1 pastes the same list into `PROTOCOL.md`; where this section and any report disagree, `BRIEF.md:27-37` wins.**

**Contract.** K2 (SIDE1's `PROTOCOL.md` and conformance suite) and K1 (SIDE0's import surface). Never another package's in-progress code.

**The five injection facts that make this possible, verified at the pin.** All 20 modules export `run(args, io: Prompter): Promise<Result<T>>`; 19 carry a typed payload (`update.ts:12` is the one `Result<void>`); **18 accept an injectable `ConfigStore`** — absent only from `src/commands/readme.ts:10` and `src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:12`, the two GitHub-Action-side verbs a UI never calls; **`process.cwd()` is read in exactly six places, all in `src/cli.ts` (`:57, :94, :95, :96, :112, :119`) and nowhere in `src/lib` or `src/commands`** — the library never asks the OS what "current" means; and `home` **must be passed explicitly** or `install.ts:238`'s POSIX suffix test misfires off POSIX and global placements land inside `~/.terum/skills/.claude/skills`. `createConfigStore(root = join(homedir(), '.terum', 'skills'), options)` at `config.ts:37` is how the sidecar points the whole library at a sandbox directory.

**Six things it must get right that are easy to miss.**

- **Every `run()` call must be wrapped in a try/catch that mirrors `src/lib/execute.ts:30-32` — this is the single most load-bearing line in the package.** `run()` **rejects**; it does not return a failing `Result` for a decline, and it does not for a closed prompt either (`PromptClosedError`, `prompt.ts:29-38`). The mapping to copy, verbatim from `execute.ts:30-32` @ `b5c0507`:
  ```ts
  try { return await run(args, io); }
  catch (error) { return { ok: false, error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) }; }
  ```
  `createExecute` at `src/lib/execute.ts:21-33` is where the CLI does this, and it belongs to **the bin** — a sidecar importing `run()` directly never goes through it. **Without this wrapper every user decline surfaces as a rejected promise, and the nine-string match below never fires.**
- **`print` is load-bearing, not decoration.** Several failures exist **only** as printed lines and never reach the `Result`: `ls.ts:59` catches a per-skill `latestTree` failure, prints it, and substitutes `'—'` in the payload. Pass a `print` callback the UI keeps.
- **`sync --hook` is a silent no-op for a team whose stamp is under an hour old** (`STAMP_FRESH_MS = 60 * 60_000`, `hook.ts:149`; gate at `sync.ts:106`). A UI must call **plain** `sync` — and the app's own sync suppresses the hook's for an hour machine-wide, which is why gap-4 D-R4 forbids a timer.
- **The one thing it cannot pass through:** `src/lib/auth.ts:44` is the **only** `stdio: 'inherit'` spawn in non-test source — `runner.run('gh', ['auth','login'], { stdio: 'inherit' })`, guarded at `:42-43` behind the confirm at `:43` ("GitHub CLI is installed but logged out. Run `gh auth login` now?"). gh's device-code flow needs a real terminal, so **that spawn cannot work through the sidecar — this is precisely an affordance the CLI cannot honour, which `BRIEF:6` sends to `GAPS.md`.** **This is now §6 decision 21, not a default this package takes alone**, because the design draws the opposite: `build.py:3539` renders `status_value(t, 'warn', 'gh is signed out') + button(t, 'Sign in', …)` and `build.py:3540`'s drawn copy reads "gh is installed but signed out. **Sign in opens gh's own login in a terminal;** Terum never asks for a token." *Default under decision 21:* honour the drawn copy where the shell can (the app opens a terminal running `gh auth login`), and file the substitute — `INV:288`'s "answer that one confirm with `false` and show a copyable instruction card" — as a `GAPS.md` line rather than silently replacing a drawn button's promised behaviour with a different, undrawn element. Either way this package answers the confirm `false` at the seam; what decision 21 settles is **what the Settings/Account board does instead**, which is Teddy's under §2.1.
- **No typed decline exists — and the nine strings are not all reached the same way.** `Result` (`result.ts:1-6`) has no discriminator for "the user pressed Cancel". **Eight of the nine sites `throw`; only `uninstallMachine.ts:58` returns `failure(...)`** — the table is in **SIDE1's assertion 4** and the same eight file:line rows apply here: `connect.ts:202`, `connect.ts:372`, `install.ts:145`, `install.ts:152`, `publish.ts:84`, `leave.ts:38`, `team.ts:121`, `team.ts:493` all `throw new Error(...)`, and `uninstallMachine.ts:58` alone is `return failure('Uninstall was cancelled.')`. *Default:* **string-match those nine AFTER the try/catch mapping above** — a raw `Result.error` match catches exactly one of the nine — and file the typed-decline ask with Ryan rather than block on it; it is an **approval on his core type**, not a task (§7).
- **`Result.ok === false` has three meanings, and `pending` is durable.** **[graft: split-by-contract C4]** A failing `Result` may mean *refused*, *declined*, or *failed with partial effect* — and `install` writes its `pending` entry (`install.ts:90`, committed at `:92`) **before** placement (`:123`), so a failed install can leave durable state the next read will show. **Therefore: no optimistic cache write for any CLI-backed mutation.** Re-read after every mutation. This is exactly the `installhalf` scenario SIDE1's conformance list asserts on.

**Five seam reads with no 1:1 CLI verb, named so nobody assumes a source.** `BRIEF:47` lists 26 `Backend` members against 20 command modules. Of the reads: `library` and `roster` are derived from `LsResult` (`ls.ts:20` — `{ local?, roster, skills }`); **`skill`, `inbox` and `catalog` have no CLI source at all** — `grep -rln "inbox\|catalog\|marketplace" src --include=*.ts` over non-test source returns only `src/commands/eval.ts` and `src/lib/evals/triggers.ts`, neither of them an inbox or a marketplace. Those three are **mock-only in phase 1** and each gets a `GAPS.md` line.

**The arithmetic, corrected.** An earlier draft wrote "23 of 26 against real data and three against the mock"; a later one wrote "20 graded / six mock-only … seven and 19", which silently counted `subscribe` and `capabilities` as graded against real CLI data and so contradicted SIDE1's own membership list. **Nine of the twenty-six have no CLI source, and the membership list is this, carried verbatim into SIDE2's *The arithmetic, corrected* so the two sections cannot print different numbers into the same `PROTOCOL.md`:** `skill`, `inbox`, `catalog` (no CLI producer at all), `openInEditor`, `copyToClipboard`, `copyImage`, `prefs` (Tauri plugin calls, blocked on J1), `subscribe` (the optional push channel) and `capabilities` (the feature-flag object). **The split to state once, in these words: 17 members are graded against real CLI data; 7 are mock-only in phase 1** (`skill`, `inbox`, `catalog`, `openInEditor`, `copyToClipboard`, `copyImage`, `prefs`)**; and 2 are adapter-native** (`subscribe` and `capabilities`) — **adapter-native means a real adapter implements them itself** (`subscribe` over the file watcher, `capabilities` from the adapter's own flags), **so they are neither graded against CLI data nor mock-only, and must not be counted in either bucket.** 17 + 7 + 2 = 26. (`copyImage` sits in the mock-only seven under decision 10's default; if the share card ever comes into scope it moves to graded and the split becomes 18 / 6 / 2.) Say exactly that in `PROTOCOL.md` rather than letting a reader assume coverage.

**Four seam members that are not verbs at all, named so they do not fall between two owners.** `INV:123-125` puts `openInEditor(path)`, `copyToClipboard(text)` and `prefs: PrefStore` under the comment "capabilities the CLI does not own" (`INV:122`), and **`copyImage` is the fourth** — member 23 of `BRIEF:47`'s 26, the PNG share card's clipboard call. They are **Tauri plugin JS calls — `plugin-opener`, `plugin-clipboard-manager`, `plugin-store` — not `run(args, io)`**, so this package's "all 20 verbs in-process, stdio only" scope does not reach them and its deliverable line must not be read as covering them silently.

- **`copyImage` is governed by §6 decision 10, whose default is that the PNG share card is out of phase-1 scope.** Under that default it ships **mock-only, with a `GAPS.md` line and no permission requested** — specifically **no `clipboard-manager.writeImage` permission and no Rust-side `fs` scope**, which is exactly what decision 10's default forbids. Implementing it from this section alone, without reading decision 10, would request both.
- **`openInEditor`, `copyToClipboard`, `prefs`: their JS half is this package's; their Rust half is J1's** (`INV:304` lists `plugin-opener`, `plugin-clipboard-manager`, `plugin-store` and `window-state` in M5's exit criterion; `window-state` is shell-level and touches no seam member). **Who owns J1 is decision 2, and it interacts with decision 3:** under **E** (the default) it is Teddy's; under **B** it moves to the Mac owner; **under B with decision 3's default — no Mac owner — J1 has no producer at all, and all three stay mock-only permanently.** So this package's dependency is stated as *whoever owns J1 having registered the three plugins*, not as "Teddy's J1". Until J1 lands, all three stay **mock-only**, each with a `GAPS.md` line, the same treatment `skill` / `inbox` / `catalog` get above.

**Deliverables.** The adapter implementing every `Backend` member over in-process `run(args, io)`, plus the four plugin-backed members above. **Where it lives:** in **its own repo** under decision 5's default — and, **after Teddy pushes `terum-skills-app`, it is re-homed into `src/backend/tauri/**`, which `INV` §10 names as M6's deliverable.** **Re-scope clause, under decision 4's non-default answer** (the app inside the CLI repo, `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills`): **this package's review path becomes spec delta → `/codex-spec` → `/codex-implement` in a worktree → `/hybrid-review`, never push to `main`, and its merge needs Ryan** — where `teniroo` is `admin: false`, some approvals are Ryan-only, and branch protection is NOT VERIFIED. That is a different package with a different gate; re-scope it before handing it out, exactly as CI2 and MAC1 say for their own non-default answers. **[graft: split-by-critical-path]** Naming that directory now closes a real hole: without it the seam directory M6 is graded on has no owner, while this package's own *must not touch* list already constrains app-repo paths.

**Plus one line in a file this package otherwise does not own, stated explicitly because M6's exit criterion is unobservable without it.** `INV:90` defines `src/backend/index.ts` as `pickBackend(): mock | tauri | http — resolved ONCE at boot`, and `INV:305` makes M6 "capability flags flip from the adapter". For the tauri adapter to be *reached* at boot, `index.ts` must gain its arm. §7 withholds `index.ts` as loop-owned; **the single `case 'tauri':` arm is carved out of that and is this package's**, delivered as a one-line PR immediately after the re-home. Everything else in `index.ts` stays the loop's. Without this sentence, `src/backend/` has two writers and nobody owns the edit M6 is graded on. **`src/backend/index.ts` is therefore a shared file across two owners — the loop and this package — and it is row 4 of §5.2's shared-file table**, resolved by sequencing: the loop lands all of `pickBackend()` in M1, this package adds the one arm afterwards. Earlier drafts asserted "no shared file crosses two owners" without naming it; it is named now.

**The line that makes it dirty if crossed.** **The moment it opens a port it stops being option O2 and becomes O1, which is the gated `ui`.** That is not a style preference: no `ui` verb exists (`grep -rn "'ui'\|command('ui" src/` → nothing), **`.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:24`** lists `ui` as Out, and **three existing vitest assertions fail the build if the string appears** — `src/commands/__tests__/setup.test.ts:246`, `:402`, `:519`, each `expect(io.lines.join('\n')).not.toMatch(/\bui\b/i)`. Unblocking it needs a build-spec rev across §2/§6.1/§12/§13, a D21 amendment (**`.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134`**) and walk Decision 2's tripwire declared met — one indivisible Ryan act that `INV:278` already defers. **Keep it stdio.**

**The other half of the same gate, so a reader sees both.** `INV:61` records that `setup` step 8 is "the line phase 2 replaces with opening the local UI", and `INV:353` records that the phase-2 gate binds **shipping a `ui` verb *and* changing `setup` step 8** together. The Day-0 ask (§5.3) deliberately excludes step 8, so **`src/commands/setup.ts`'s step 8 is unchanged in phase 1 and the tripwire stays unspent** — that is **§6 decision 17**, and a different answer there would relax this package's stdio-only constraint and add a CLI package.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** A vitest suite driving each verb through a scripted prompter against **bare-repo fixtures and a sandboxed `ConfigStore` root** — the shape the CLI's own tests use (`AGENTS.md:64-65`, "bare-repo fixtures, no network"), and specifically the fixture-clone recipe at `receiptCheck.test.ts:24-49` (git init, seed a skill, commit a receipt at the exact tree hash). Plus a frame round-trip per `Prompter` member. Plus three adversarial cases: an unexpected question, a decline vs a failure, a cancelled `Run<T>`. Plus **SIDE1's conformance suite green against the real adapter**, and contract tests asserting every `receipts` return `receiptSchema.parse`s and matches FE1's fixtures field for field. `validate` is driven with `args.cwd` set to a team checkout (`validate.ts:19-29`) so the sidecar never touches the user's real `ConfigStore`.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: **CLI1** for the typed bare import (until then it can deep-import `terum-skills/dist/commands/*.js` untyped, which works today and is exactly the door CLI1's `exports` map closes — so it can **start** immediately and **ship** after); **SIDE0**, **SIDE1**, **CLI3** (else it becomes the fifth receipt reader), **FE1** (fixture shape). Unblocks: M6.

**Must not touch.** The CLI repo (nothing in `terum-skills`); in the app repo, `src/screens/**` and `src/components/**`. **The rule `BRIEF:47` actually states, verbatim:** "Nothing outside `src/backend/` imports @tauri-apps/* or does I/O; a vitest test greps the tree and an eslint `no-restricted-imports` rule enforces it. No component branches on `isTauri()`." **That is the binding constraint.** The narrower allow-list this plan would like on top of it — that `src/screens/**` and `src/components/**` import only from `src/backend/types` and never from `src/backend/mock/**`, `src/backend/tauri/**` or `src/fixtures/**` — is **this plan's proposal, pending M1's spec**, not a quoted rule; obey the `BRIEF:47` sentence, and treat the allow-list as the shape to expect. Also off limits: `src/backend/index.ts` beyond the single `case 'tauri':` arm named above. **Plus the standing rules, which are what make "after the re-home" mean something concrete:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`); *(b)* **the public `terum-skills-app` does not exist until Teddy creates it and pushes** — the local repo has no remote, and `TH:61` tells teammates not to clone or recreate it — so **this package is built in the sidecar's own repo against the CLI's types at the pin, and the re-home into `src/backend/tauri/**` plus the one-line `index.ts` PR happen only after that push**; *(c)* **no printed string changes** (`BRIEF.md:28`; `TH:61`) — including the one this package is most tempted by: the drawn Settings **Sign in** button and its caption stay as drawn, this package answers the `gh auth login` confirm `false` at the seam, and what the board does instead is decision 21 and Teddy's.

**And the flag list it may branch on is closed.** `capabilities()` carries exactly the five named booleans the app repo's `GAPS.md:28-32` lists — `disablePerMachine`, `inboxEventLog`, `offtargetKind`, `machineRegistry`, `perCaseEvalTables` — from `BRIEF.md:6`'s four authorized hard gaps (the Inbox event log and `offtarget` counted as one there, named separately in code), plus the three non-gap probes `windowChrome`, `openInEditor`, `clipboard`. **Everything else is built as if real, mock or not.** A seam member with no CLI source — `skill`, `inbox`, `catalog`, and `openInEditor` / `copyToClipboard` / `prefs` / `copyImage` until their plugins exist — ships **mock-only with a `GAPS.md` line**, never behind a new flag.

**Blocking Teddy decisions, with their defaults.** **2 — E or B for M5; default E**, which puts J1 with Teddy; **under B the three plugin-backed members' producer moves to the Mac owner, and under B with decision 3's default (no Mac owner) they have no producer at all and stay mock-only permanently.** **4 — repo placement for the app; default: public sibling `terum-skills-app`**; the non-default answer re-scopes this package's review path into Ryan's pipeline (see *Deliverables*). **5 — sidecar repo placement; default: its own repo**, re-homed after the push. **10 — is the PNG share card in phase-1 scope; default: no**, so `copyImage` is mock-only and this package requests **no** `clipboard-manager.writeImage` permission and **no** Rust-side `fs` scope. **12 — the sidecar runtime; default: require a global `npm install -g terum-skills`.** **16 — the seam path; default `src/backend/tauri/`**, which names its re-home target and its `no-restricted-imports` boundary. **17 — `setup` step 8; default: not yet**, which is what keeps this package stdio-only. **19 — is this role staffed; default: no**, in which case the package is unowned and §5.1 link 3 has no owner. **21 — the drawn gh Sign in button; default: honour the drawn copy and file the instruction card as a `GAPS.md` line** (see the sixth bullet above). Plus the non-decision dependency: **J1's owner having registered `plugin-opener`, `plugin-clipboard-manager` and `plugin-store`** before `openInEditor`, `copyToClipboard` and `prefs` can leave the mock. **The one package-internal default left, now that gh-auth is decision 21:** string-match the nine decline strings, after the `execute.ts:30-32` mapping.

### SIDE3 — The `externalBin` sidecar packaging spike

**Owner role:** CI owner — **unnamed — `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` assigns this work to the Mac teammates (`TH:12`, `:13`, `:43`); Teddy names the individual** (§6 decision 19, re-scoped: the open question is *which* teammate, not whether teammates exist — `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who have already received it). **The CI owner runs the check; the backend developer reviews the result and does not run it.** **Size:** M. **Start today:** no — **day 2-3, as soon as CI1 is green.** **[graft: split-by-critical-path]**

**Goal and why it matters.** Close the runtime question by measurement, not by reading. As posed — "Node SEA vs bun vs require node" — it is **not a work package**: it has an owner and a contract of sorts but **no acceptance check anyone can run today**, because sidecar `externalBin` packaging and any `#[tauri::command]` / `invoke` round trip **were never exercised on this box** — `TB:249` item 9's own words, which an earlier draft widened to "never exercised anywhere in the evidence set" (the evidence set does contain observed third-party `tauri-action` runs, `TB:247`). `TB:103` names it as option E's own "cannot prove". Reshaped, it becomes checkable: **package the packed `terum-skills` tarball plus a thin stdio `main` into a Tauri bundle and prove one round trip.** *(Not hello-world: the bounds below — `engines.node`, a `proper-lockfile` that actually runs, `node:child_process` — are what make the size answer useful, and a hello-world binary satisfies none of them. An earlier draft said hello-world in the goal and `Prompter` in the acceptance; they are now the same experiment.)* The three-way recommendation is the spike's *output*, not its input.

**The critical-path move.** It does **not** have to wait for M5. **CI1 produces a real Tauri bundle in week 1 using the existing probe.** Run the spike against that bundle and the biggest M6 unknown is answered in week 1 instead of at M6, when the whole architecture depends on it.

**Inputs to open on day one.** CI1's green run and its Release assets; **the five probe files, from the scratch public repo CI1 commits them into** — they originate at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/probe/`, inside the design folder, so §2.1 makes publishing them a named Teddy act and CI1 is where they land; and a checkout at the pin for `package.json:11-13`, `src/lib/runner.ts:1` and `src/lib/config.ts:53`. **Two shorthands, expanded once so this section stands alone:** `cli-libs.v2.md:554` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/cli-libs.v2.md`, inside the design folder, which is **not a git repo and exists as one copy** — the one fact this package takes from it (deep import works today *because* there is no `exports` map) is restated in CLI1 and in SIDE0, so do not go looking for the file. `TB:249` item 9 = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-tauri-build.md`, an uncommitted file **Teddy attaches to the ask**; the item reads, verbatim: "Sidecar `externalBin` packaging and any `#[tauri::command]` / `invoke` round trip **were never exercised on this box**."

**Contract / bounds the answer must respect.** `package.json:11-13` `engines.node >= 22.12.0`; the four runtime deps (`commander`, `proper-lockfile`, `yaml`, `zod`) — **`proper-lockfile` must actually run**, it holds the config lock at `config.ts:53`; and the library needs `node:child_process` (`runner.ts:1` is `import { spawn } from 'node:child_process'`), `node:fs/promises`, `node:os`, `node:crypto` — so **no webview-only shape qualifies**. Costs on record, **with the citations an earlier draft left off — they are the whole framing of the recommendation, so they must be checkable**: **~116 MB/platform for Node SEA** (`tauri-feasibility.md:403` — "the SEA *is* a copy of the Node binary plus the blob. Measured on this machine: `/usr/bin/node` = **120,702,160 bytes (116 MB)**"), at **Stability 1.1 — Active development** (`:393`, and the comparison table's stability row at `:455`), with **macOS x64 unsupported** (`:880` R7: "Node SEA is Stability 1.1 (active development) and macOS **x64 is not supported**"); **~57 MB for bun** (`:414` — hello-world at Bun 1.1.30, `bun-darwin-arm64`); the size row of the comparison table is `:446`. *(Those cites are `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/tauri-feasibility.md`, which is in no repo — so the numbers are quoted here rather than pointed at. `INV:158` and `INV:256` carry the same figures if Teddy attaches `INV` instead, where **`INV` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-investigation.md`**, likewise an uncommitted file on his box and in no repo. **None of them is in `TB`** — a grep returns nothing — which is why an owner handed only `TB` could not check them.)* The third option is to require a global install — which is now a first-class supported shape, `invite`'s Slack block leads with it: **`src/commands/invite.ts:49` is `export const GLOBAL_INSTALL = 'npm install -g terum-skills';` and `:53-54`'s `slackBlock()` emits `GLOBAL_INSTALL` as the first line inside the fenced block, before `joinCommand(...)`.** (`invite.ts` is 67 lines at the pin; an earlier draft cited `:304-310`, which does not exist.)

**Deliverable.** A written three-way recommendation with size and install story, backed by a bundle that ran, **plus the on-disk size of that bundle under each runtime option actually attempted** — the size number is the deliverable, and it is only meaningful for a bundle carrying the real library.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** A bundle that launches, spawns the sidecar, and completes **one `print` frame plus one `confirm` round trip against a scripted answer** — i.e. one `Prompter` round trip over the real packed CLI, not a stub — **on the CI owner's own desktop OS, named in the deliverable, with the bundle's on-disk size recorded per runtime option.** The question being answered (`externalBin` packaging plus one `invoke` round trip) is OS-agnostic, so answering it on Linux or Windows x64 beats not answering it, and CI1's matrix carries a Linux target for exactly this reason. **Per-OS caveat that must ship with the answer:** macOS packaging remains unproven until a Mac exists (decision 3), so a green Linux or Windows spike settles the *architecture* question and not the macOS one.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: **CI1** having produced a bundle the spike can package into; **Teddy publishing the five probe files** (§2.1) so CI1 exists at all; and **a machine running one of CI1's target OSes** — the Linux target is in the matrix so this is the CI owner's own box rather than an unverified Mac. If none of the three holds, say so in one line and decision 12 falls to its default, the same honest treatment MAC1 gets. Unblocks: decision 12, and M6's shipping shape.

**Must not touch.** `src-tauri/**` of the real app (J1's, and Teddy's under option E) — **and note there is none to touch: `BRIEF.md:15` says "src-tauri is NOT created tonight" and `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:79` says the M5 shell "is empty by design", so the only Tauri artifact that exists is the preserved probe CI1 commits.** Also the CLI repo. **Plus the standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`) — the five probe files reach you by Teddy publishing them into CI1's scratch repo or attaching them, never by reading his design folder; *(b)* **the public `terum-skills-app` does not exist until Teddy pushes** (`TH:14`, `:61`), so this spike packages into **CI1's scratch-repo bundle** and nothing else; *(c)* **no printed string changes** — and specifically **not the one `TH:57` appears to hand this package**: "whether the app ever prints the bare `terum-skills <verb>` form or keeps the boards' `npx -y terum-skills@latest` spelling" is a **printed string**, therefore Teddy's design-side call (`BRIEF.md:32`: print the npx spelling everywhere, never the bare form, no launch detection, no toggle; `GAPS.md:13`). **Report the runtime and the install story; do not choose a spelling.**

**The files it DOES touch, which are CI1's — named rather than denied.** An `externalBin` sidecar cannot be added without editing the scratch repo's **`src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`** (`bundle.externalBin`) and **`src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`** (the shell/sidecar permission) — two of the five probe files CI1 commits as its first commit — plus a **`.github/workflows/**`** step that builds the sidecar binary before `tauri build`, and CI1 claims that directory. **CI1 and this package are both lane D and both the CI owner, so this is one owner, not two** — but it is a shared file and §5.2's table now carries it. **The resolution: sequence CI1 → SIDE3, and do this work on a branch that never lands on the tag CI1's green run was cut from**, so CI1's acceptance artifact stays reproducible.

**Blocking Teddy decisions.** **12 — the sidecar runtime.** **Its default matters and must be stated, because this package's acceptance may never run:** if the spike does not happen, **require `npm install -g terum-skills`** — the shape `src/commands/invite.ts:49`/`:53-54` already leads with — and revisit when a bundle exists. Without that fallback on the record, a silent Teddy leaves M6's shipping shape undefined.

**Review path.** The scratch repo. No production repo trip.

### CI1 — Tauri build and release workflow in a scratch PUBLIC repo

**Owner role:** CI owner — **unnamed — `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` assigns this work to the Mac teammates (`TH:12`, `:13`, `:43`); Teddy names the individual** (§6 decision 19, re-scoped: the open question is *which* teammate, not whether teammates exist — `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who have already received it); the package is written without a name rather than assigned to one nobody has supplied. **Size:** M. **Start today:** yes — it needs a GitHub account and **the five probe files, which only Teddy can publish** (§2.1, minutes) — **provided decisions 3, 4, 10 and 11 stand at their defaults**, which they do unless he says otherwise; no *further* decision from him beyond publishing the files.

**Goal and why it matters.** Produce, from a machine that is **not** a Mac, an artifact a Mac owner can open. That is option E's entire premise, and nothing else in the plan tests it.

**Why it is clean.** It uses the **existing Tauri probe** as a stand-in app, so it needs **no `terum-skills-app` code and no screen, board or fixture from the design** — the one thing it does need out of the design folder is the five probe files themselves, and §2.1 makes publishing those a named Teddy act rather than a silent dependency. It runs entirely in a scratch repo that is **not** `terum-skills-app` — Teddy creates that one. It owns `.github/workflows/**` in that scratch repo, **disjoint from every other *owner's* files** — not from every other package's: **SIDE3 is the same owner and the same lane, and it edits that repo's `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` and `.github/workflows/**` to add the `externalBin` sidecar.** That overlap is real, it is named in §5.2's shared-file table, and it is resolved by sequencing CI1 → SIDE3 with SIDE3 on a branch that never lands on the tag CI1's green run was cut from. **Its first commit should be the complete scaffolded app** — see the recipe below — so SIDE3 and MAC1 clone one pinned artifact instead of independently scaffolding two.

**What the preserved probe actually is, before anyone runs anything.** It lives at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/probe/` — **inside the design folder, which is not a git repo and exists as one copy on Teddy's box**, so it reaches nobody until he publishes it (§2.1 names that as his act, and this package depends on it). And it is **a `cargo check` fixture, not a bundleable app.** Verified contents: `Cargo.toml`, `build.rs`, `lib.rs`, `tauri.conf.json`, `capabilities.default.json`, plus `env.sh`, `run-check.sh`, `README.txt`, `tauri-info.txt`, `tauri.conf.json.typo` and `logs/`. It is missing everything a bundle needs:

- **no `package.json`**, while its own `tauri.conf.json` declares `beforeDevCommand: "npm run dev"` and `beforeBuildCommand: "npm run build"`;
- **no `../dist`**, which `build.frontendDist` points at;
- **no `src/main.rs`** — only `lib.rs` was preserved, and `Cargo.toml` declares `[lib] name = "app_lib"` with **no `[[bin]]`**;
- **no `icons/`**, though `bundle.icon` lists five files and `bundle.targets` is `"all"`;
- **no `src-tauri/` layout**, which `tauri-action` expects — only a subset of the real project was preserved. Precisely, because the two files say different amounts: `README.txt` is one line and records only the **root** ("disposable probe root: `/home/teniroo/.cache/tauri-build-probe`"), while **`run-check.sh` is what names the project — its `cd /home/teniroo/.cache/tauri-build-probe/proj/src-tauri || exit 99`** — and that script runs **`cargo check`**, not a build. Neither path exists on anyone else's machine.

**The recipe — and it is run ONCE, here, not twice.** `npm create tauri-app` to scaffold a minimal app, then **overwrite** `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `src-tauri/build.rs`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` and `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` with the preserved probe files, generate icons with `npx tauri icon`, and keep the scaffold's `package.json`, its stub `index.html` and its `src-tauri/src/main.rs` bin entry. Without those three the scaffold has nothing to open. Ten minutes, but it is ten minutes nobody budgeted.

**Then commit the whole thing** — scaffold + the five overwritten probe files + the generated `icons/` + a committed `package-lock.json` — as this package's first commit. **That is what makes CI1's artifact and MAC1's window the same artifact.** An earlier draft handed the identical recipe to CI1 *and* to MAC1 step 1 with nothing pinning them to the same Tauri CLI, Rust or scaffold-template version — and MAC1's deliverable includes "the Tauri and Rust versions used", which CI1's asset matrix and M1's config set are supposed to consume. Two independently scaffolded apps make the Mac verdict describe a different artifact than CI builds, which is exactly the ambiguity C1 exists to settle. **With the scaffold committed here, MAC1 step 1 collapses to `git clone` + `npm install && npm run tauri dev`**, reporting the versions the repo already pins; MAC1 keeps the long recipe only as the fallback for the case where this package does not exist (decision 4 vetoed toward the CLI repo, or decision 19 leaving the CI owner unstaffed).

**One value to set before committing, and it is the only edit to a preserved probe file this plan authorizes:** the probe ships `"trafficLightPosition": { "x": 16, "y": 18 }`, while C2's drawn target is `{ x: 16, y: 14 }` (`TB:178`). **`14` is the authoritative number and it is arithmetic, not taste:** the field addresses the light group's **top-left**, and the drawing centres the lights on **y = 20 in a 40 px bar** with **12 px** dots (`TB:171`, deriving it from the design's own `build.py:173`, `:213`, `:223`), so 20 − 12/2 = 14; `18` matches no drawn value and is simply what the probe file happens to carry. **Set `y` to 14 in the committed `tauri.conf.json`** and note the change in the commit message, so a C2 delta measured on the Mac is a macOS finding rather than an artifact of the probe file. See MAC1 step 1.

**Inputs to open on day one.** `tauri-apps/tauri-action`'s `action.yml` at tag **`action-v1.0.0`**; the five probe files from `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/probe/`, published by Teddy (§2.1) — attach them to the ask or push them straight into this repo; `TB:241-261` (the NOT VERIFIED list, especially items 8, 15, 16, 18 — the list is numbered so item *N* is on line 240 + *N*); `TB` §§4-6. **`TB` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-tauri-build.md`, an uncommitted file on Teddy's box that he attaches to this ask — it is in no repo** (verified at the pin: `.planning/research/` holds only the three eval documents). Expanded here as well as in the citation block, because a pasted section carries no header.

**Contract.** K7 (§3.5). **Not** Tauri's CI docs page, which carries at least two stale statements — the auto-initialization sentence (removed in v1.0.0) and an "only available in public repos" comment on `ubuntu-22.04-arm` (**`TB:255`** item 15). **Diff the docs page against the `action-v1.0.0` README; do not copy it.** Renames to carry over (**`TB:256`** item 16): `includeUpdaterJson` → `uploadUpdaterJson`; `assetNamePattern` → `releaseAssetNamePattern`; `includeRelease`, `includeDebug`, `updaterJsonKeepUniversal` are **gone**.

**The failure mode that makes the artifact-existence gate mandatory, not polish** (`TB:32`). `tauri-bundler` compiles its `macos` module only under `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` **on the host**, so on Linux `MacOsBundle` and `Dmg` fall through to the catch-all arm, log `"ignoring app"`, and **the build exits 0 having produced nothing** — `TB:32`'s own words, sourced there to `crates/tauri-bundler/src/bundle.rs:8-11,166-198`. A green run with an empty Release is the default failure here, and **`TB:227` names the mitigation: "assert on the presence of `.app`/`.dmg` paths, or use tauri-action's `artifactPaths` output as a gate"**, `artifactPaths` being a documented output of `action.yml`.

**Deliverable — the workflow, from the `[veto cheap]` defaults. Every bullet carries its `TB` anchor, because `TB` is the only document this owner has and this plan's standing rule is that every claim cites a line.**
- **Matrix:** `macos-latest` with **both** `--target aarch64-apple-darwin` **and** `--target x86_64-apple-darwin` (until the Mac's architecture is known — decision 3), plus **`windows-11-vs2026-arm` pinned explicitly**, because the `windows-11-arm` label swaps to the VS2026 image in September 2026 (**`TB:228`**).
- **Plus `ubuntu-22.04` producing `.AppImage` and `.deb`.** Not for shipping — **so SIDE3 has a bundle its own owner can launch.** Without it the only bundles this workflow makes run on macOS (blocked by decision 3's no-Mac-owner default) or Windows-on-ARM (Teddy's own laptop, which §2.1 keeps), and SIDE3's acceptance has no machine. The Linux job also catches a `tauri.conf.json` mistake in two minutes instead of eight.
  - **The Linux leg needs its own apt step — `tauri-action` does NOT install Linux system dependencies.** The package list below is **quoted, not invented**, and its one checkable anchor on this box is `tauri-feasibility.md:703`, which records the Tauri pipelines docs page as fetched this session: "`tauri-apps/tauri-action@v1` … matrix of **macos-latest (`--target aarch64-apple-darwin`)**, **macos-latest (`--target x86_64-apple-darwin`)**, **ubuntu-22.04**, **ubuntu-22.04-arm**, **windows-latest**. **Ubuntu step installs `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libappindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf xdg-utils`.**" (`tauri-feasibility.md` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/tauri-feasibility.md`, **inside the design folder and in no repo** — which is why the sentence is quoted here rather than pointed at.) Copy it:
    ```yaml
    - name: install dependencies (ubuntu only)
      if: matrix.platform == 'ubuntu-22.04'
      run: |
        sudo apt-get update
        sudo apt-get install -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libappindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf xdg-utils
    ```
    **Two NOT VERIFIED clauses ride with it, and both must survive into the deliverable.** (a) That this exact list is sufficient on the `ubuntu-22.04` image *today*: no workflow was run for this app (see the honesty clauses below), so the list is the upstream recommendation rather than an observed green run. (b) **The anchor is Tauri's own CI docs page, and `TB:255` item 15 says that page "carr[ies] at least two stale statements" and to "diff the docs page against the `action-v1.0.0` README, do not copy it"** — the two staleness examples it names are the auto-initialization sentence and an "only available in public repos" comment on `ubuntu-22.04-arm`, *neither of them this apt step*, but the caution applies to the page as a whole. **Do the diff against the `action-v1.0.0` README on day one and correct the list if it differs; no copy of that README exists on Teddy's box, so this plan could not do the diff for you.** **`TB:62`'s "nine documented deps" is about Teddy's local WSL box and does not transfer to a runner** — do not use it here. Since SIDE3's acceptance runs on this leg, **SIDE3's dependency on CI1 is conditional on this step working**, and that is visible rather than assumed.
- **NSIS, not MSI** — the arm64 image has no WiX (**`TB:228`**). The NSIS stub itself is x86 and runs under WoA emulation while the app binary is native arm64 (same line); say so rather than promise a `.msi`.
- **WebView2 in the default `downloadBootstrapper` mode, never `offlineInstaller`** — `arch == "arm64"` silently falls through to the x86 offline-installer URL in the bundler (**`TB:248`** item 8: read from source, never observed, no upstream issue; **`TB:228`** carries the same default).
- **`signingIdentity: "-"` (ad-hoc) — required, not optional** (**`TB:226`**). Without it Tauri never calls `codesign` at all, the binary carries only the linker's ad-hoc signature with `Sealed Resources=none`, and a downloaded bundle produces the **"damaged, Move to Trash"** verdict with no documented way forward. What it does **not** buy, in Tauri's own words quoted at `TB:226`: "Ad-hoc code signing does not prevent MacOS from requiring users to whitelist the installation in their Privacy & Security settings."
- **`uploadUpdaterJson: false`** — it defaults to **true** in v1.0.0, so the recommended workflow would publish a `latest.json` and **silently make GitHub Releases the app's updater endpoint** — the exact decision TF R12 reserves for Teddy (**`TB:230`**; decision 11).
- **Do not ship a `.dmg`** (**`TB:145`**) — quarantine survives the drag to `/Applications`, and the CI dmg step is flaky and needs `retryAttempts`.
- `GITHUB_TOKEN` with **write** workflow permissions — default token permissions are read-only and must be enabled under Actions → Workflow permissions. **NOT FOUND in `TB`:** a grep for `GITHUB_TOKEN` and "Workflow permissions" over `TB` returns nothing. Its actual source is the same fetched docs page as the apt step above — `tauri-feasibility.md:703`, which reads: `tauri-action` "[p]asses `GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}` and **warns that default token permissions are read-only — enable write under Actions → Workflow permissions**" — carried second-hand at `repo-and-house-facts.md:80-82`, which quotes it as `TF:703`. Both files are inside the design folder and reach nobody, so the sentence is quoted here. Treat it as a step to confirm in the GitHub UI on day one, not as an observed fact.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** A green run whose **artifact-existence gate passes** and whose **Release** — not a workflow artifact; a Release asset downloads anonymously, a workflow artifact returns **401** without a login — carries both `.app.tar.gz` assets plus an arm64 NSIS `-setup.exe`; and anonymous `curl` of a Release asset returns 200.

**Three honesty clauses that must survive into the deliverable.** (1) **No workflow was run for this app in the session that produced `TB`** (`TB:131`, whose exact words are "No workflow was run for this app **this session**" — an earlier draft of this document widened that to "in any session", which is a stronger claim than the source makes and would have shipped inside this package's own output). Every timing on record is other public repos' warm-cache builds, so the first build here will be cold; expect the top of the 8-15-minute planning band (`TB:131`). (2) The zero-credential proof (`formulahendry/acp-ui`, `macos-latest`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` only, zero `APPLE_*` vars, shipping an aarch64 `.dmg` and `.app.tar.gz`) ran **`tauri-action@v0`** while this recommends **`@v1`**, whose v1.0.0 breaking changes no observed run covers (`TB:131`; the run itself is `TB:247`). (3) **The Linux apt step is the upstream recommendation, not an observed green run** — see the `ubuntu-22.04` bullet.

**Cost** (**`TB:86`**). Free and unlimited on a **public** repo, macOS included. On a private repo a macOS job bills **$0.062/min against 2,000 included Free minutes** — roughly **13 to 25 single-target builds a month** at the 8-15-minute planning band. That is why public is the default (decision 4).

### CI2 — Production release workflow and `HANDOFF-M5.md`

**Owner role:** CI owner — the same person as CI1, and **unnamed — `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` assigns this work to the Mac teammates (`TH:12`, `:13`, `:43`); Teddy names the individual** (§6 decision 19, re-scoped: the open question is *which* teammate, not whether teammates exist — `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who have already received it). **Size:** M. **Start today:** no — after Teddy pushes `terum-skills-app`. **It is delivered in two halves, CI2a and CI2b, because only the first is acceptable by the owner alone.** **[graft: split-by-critical-path W3-REL]**

**Goal and why it matters.** CI1 deliberately stops at the scratch repo. Without this package, **nobody owns re-homing the workflow into the real app**, MAC1's step 2 depends on "CI1's tarball" that no producer makes for the real app, and the Mac owner ends up writing their own install instructions. This is the one genuinely *unassigned* item the winning partition left, as opposed to the items §7 withholds on purpose.

**Inputs to open on day one.** The workflow proven in CI1; `terum-skills-app` once pushed; **the blank seventeen-row checklist, reproduced in full inside this section below** — an earlier draft sourced it as "the blank table reproduced in the MAC1 section below (`TB:175-193`)", which is a pointer into another package's section plus a pointer into a document that is in no repo, and CI2 and MAC1 are **different roles** (§5.2 lanes D and E). Under decision 3's default the Mac owner does not exist at all, so **the person who must ship the table is the one person guaranteed not to have it.** It is therefore carried here, not cited. `TB:241-261` items 3, 5, 18 (item *N* is on line 240 + *N*, so those are `TB:243`, `TB:245`, `TB:258`); **`TB` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-tauri-build.md`, attached to the ask by Teddy — it is in no repo.** **Not MAC1's filled table** — an earlier draft listed that here too and made the two packages mutually blocking: `HANDOFF-M5.md` must ship the **empty** table for a Mac owner to fill, and filling it is MAC1 step 2, which depends on this package's Release. Empty table in, filled table out.

**Contract.** K7 (§3.5), unchanged from CI1 except for the repository it runs in.

**Deliverables, in two halves — CI2a is acceptable by the owner alone; CI2b is not.**

**CI2a — the workflow re-homed and the hand-off document, accepted on push.**
1. The proven workflow, re-homed into `terum-skills-app` — same matrix, same ad-hoc `signingIdentity: "-"`, same `uploadUpdaterJson: false`, same artifact-existence gate, same Linux apt step.
2. **`HANDOFF-M5.md` at the repo root** (contents below).
**CI2a's acceptance:** the workflow lints / dry-runs, and `HANDOFF-M5.md` is present at the repo root and self-consistent — its seventeen-row table is the blank one, and every asset-URL *pattern* it names matches what the workflow's `releaseAssetNamePattern` will produce. **No green run is required for CI2a**, which is the point of the split.

**CI2b — the first green Release of the real app. Gated on Teddy's J1, and it says so.**
3. A green run against the real app whose artifact-existence gate passes and whose **Release** carries both `.app.tar.gz` assets plus an arm64 NSIS `-setup.exe`, with anonymous `curl` of a Release asset returning **200**. **This is J3-for-the-real-app (`TB:28`), and it is impossible until `terum-skills-app` contains a `src-tauri/` that `cargo check`s — i.e. until J1 lands.** §2.1 keeps J1 with Teddy under decision 2's default E, and decision 18's default leaves M5 at build position five, *after* M2-M4 — so **CI2b can sit idle for weeks after CI2a is done, and that is expected rather than a stall to escalate.** This package's own *must not touch* forbids it creating `src-tauri/**`, so it cannot unblock itself.

`HANDOFF-M5.md` contains: the **two-line install** — `curl -L -o terum-skills.app.tar.gz "<release asset URL>"` then `tar -xzf … && mv "<the .app>" /Applications/`; the **C1-C17 table reproduced verbatim from the block below** so the Mac owner needs no other document; the rule in one line — **"you may change nothing in `tauri.conf.json` — report a delta"**; the **"if the window is blank" note** (below); **no `.dmg`**; and the **quarantine warning**: downloading in Safari and double-clicking in Finder makes Archive Utility **propagate** quarantine and lands the reader in the five-step ritual — double-click, blocked, System Settings, Privacy & Security, scroll, Open Anyway, confirm, type the login password. Control-click-and-Open is gone. Escape hatch `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/<the app>"`; whether that needs `sudo` is ownership-dependent and **NOT VERIFIED** (**`TB:258`** item 18). **Apple states each half of the curl+tar claim separately; the combined end-to-end path was never observed on a Mac.**

**The seventeen blank rows `HANDOFF-M5.md` must carry, reproduced here so this package needs no other section and no other document** (from `TB:175-193`; the same table MAC1 walks, and the two must stay byte-identical — if one is edited, edit both):

| # | Check | Pass condition | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | macOS window has `titleBarStyle: "Overlay"` **and** `decorations: true` | both present; not `decorations: false` | |
| C2 | `trafficLightPosition` is set in **logical** px | `{ x: 16, y: 14 }` puts the group's top-left where the drawing does | |
| C3 | Lights land at the drawn inset | leftmost light's left edge 16 px from the window's left; group centred on y = 20 px in a 40 px bar | |
| C4 | Light diameter vs the drawing | drawn dots are 12 px; **measure** the OS-drawn ones and record the delta (native size NOT VERIFIED) | *measurement, not pass/fail* |
| C5 | Light spacing vs the drawing | drawn pitch is 20 px (12 + 8); **measure** the OS pitch, record the delta | *measurement, not pass/fail* |
| C6 | Nothing collides with the lights | the Terum mark's 24×24 box begins at x = 76 px; nothing paints left of it on macOS | |
| C7 | Overlay bar height vs the drawn bar | drawn bar is 40 px; `Overlay` has OS-dependent height variations, so record the actual height and whether the row still centres the search field | |
| C8 | Hidden title | `hiddenTitle: true`; the design draws no window title anywhere | |
| C9 | Drag region works on the bar background | dragging the empty area of the bar moves the window | |
| C10 | Drag region does not silently fail on children | back/forward, the 480 px search field and the bell are each explicitly drag-enabled or explicitly interactive; no dead element | |
| C11 | Double-click-to-zoom | double-clicking the bar background zooms/unzooms; under `Overlay` plus a custom drag region this is hand-implemented via `startDragging()` | |
| C12 | Dragging while unfocused | click-drag on an unfocused window still moves it, or the limitation is accepted and written down | |
| C13 | Min size | `minWidth`/`minHeight` set; at minimum the 240 + 480 + 240 = 960 px clusters do not overlap | |
| C14 | Full-screen | entering full screen keeps the lights positioned and leaves no gap; leaving restores | *observation task, NOT VERIFIED* |
| C15 | Light and dark theme | both render the same hard-coded light fills against the theme's `chrome` background; check contrast in Light | |
| C16 | Three-mode header component | macOS OS lights plus reserved gutter; Windows/Linux drawn controls with `decorations: false`; browser/mock the cosmetic drawn lights | |
| C17 | Undecorated resize (Windows/Linux leg) | resizing an undecorated window is smooth; 2.11.4 shipped resize fixes for undecorated windows, so test it | |

**One note that must ship beside that table, because `HANDOFF-M5.md` carries the same blank rows MAC1 walks.** The preserved Tauri probe ships `"trafficLightPosition": { "x": 16, "y": 18 }` (verified in the probe's own `tauri.conf.json`), while **C2's drawn value is `{ x: 16, y: 14 }`** (`TB:178`). **`14` is authoritative, and the derivation belongs in the note so the Mac owner does not have to take it on faith:** the field addresses the light group's **top-left**, the drawing centres the lights on **y = 20 in a 40 px bar** with **12 px** dots (`TB:171`), so 20 − 12/2 = 14. CI1 sets `y` to 14 in the committed scaffold for exactly this reason. **Write it into `HANDOFF-M5.md` in one line: "a C2 delta of 4 px against the probe file's own value is not a macOS finding — the app's committed value is 14 (the group's top-left, 20 − 6, per the drawn 40 px bar); record the value the config actually carries alongside the measurement."**

**Acceptance.** **CI2a (CI owner alone, today, no green run needed):** the re-homed workflow lints / dry-runs, `HANDOFF-M5.md` is present at the repo root, its seventeen-row table is the blank one above, its asset-URL patterns match the workflow's `releaseAssetNamePattern`, and it carries the C2 note. **All of that runs on the CI owner's own machine and completes CI2a.** **CI2b (gated on J1, not on the owner):** a green run against the real app whose **artifact-existence gate passes**, a **Release** carrying non-empty assets — both `.app.tar.gz`, an arm64 NSIS `-setup.exe` — and anonymous `curl` of a Release asset returning **200**.

**Deferred confirmation, explicitly not a gate.** "A Mac owner completes the two-line install without asking a question." **Under decision 3's default there is no Mac owner, and CI2a completes without it** — an earlier draft made it part of the acceptance, which under the plan's own default meant CI2 could never be accepted. Until someone does it, that row reads **UNVERIFIED**, and the honest statement that must ship with `HANDOFF-M5.md` is: the `.dmg`-free curl-plus-tar path and the quarantine ritual are **untested end to end** — §8 already records that Apple states each half separately and the combined path was never observed on a Mac (`TB:258` item 18).

**Depends on / unblocks.** **CI2a depends on:** **CI1** (a proven workflow) and **Teddy pushing `terum-skills-app`** (the hinge). **CI2b additionally depends on Teddy's J1 — `tauri init` and a `src-tauri/` that `cargo check`s inside `terum-skills-app`** — which §2.1 keeps with Teddy under decision 2's default E and which decision 18's default leaves at build position five, after M2-M4. An earlier draft omitted J1 from this line while §5.1 sequenced CI2 as "hours after link 5" (link 5 *being* J1/J2), a contradiction that would have handed this package out as owner-acceptable and then stalled it on Rust work nobody scheduled. Unblocks: **MAC1 step 2** against the real app (from CI2b).

**Must not touch.** `src-tauri/**` (J1's) — **which is why CI2b cannot unblock itself, and the reason is on the record twice: `BRIEF.md:15` keeps `src-tauri` out of the loop's build entirely, and `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:79` describes the post-push M5 shell as "empty by design"**; `src/` anywhere in the app repo. **Plus the standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`); *(b)* **this package is the first one that legitimately lives in `terum-skills-app`, and it therefore starts only after Teddy creates the repo and pushes** (`TH:14`, `:79`) — before that there is nothing to re-home into, and CI2a must not be started against a clone that does not exist; *(c)* **no printed string changes** (`BRIEF.md:28`; `TH:61`) — `HANDOFF-M5.md` is a new document, not an edit to any drawn copy.

**Blocking Teddy decisions, with their defaults.** **2 — E or B for M5; default E**, under which J1 is Teddy's and CI2b waits on him. **4 — repo placement for the app and its CI; default: public sibling `terum-skills-app`**; under the non-default answer — the app inside the CLI repo — **this package becomes Ryan's**, not the CI owner's, because visibility is admin-only. **11 — is GitHub Releases the app's updater endpoint; default: no**, `uploadUpdaterJson: false`. **18 — pull M5 ahead of M2-M4; default: no, leave it at position five**, which is what puts weeks between CI2a and CI2b. **19 — is the CI-owner role staffed; default: no**, in which case both halves are unowned.

**Review path.** Ordinary PRs against `terum-skills-app`. If it lands inside the CLI repo instead: spec delta → `/codex-spec` → `/codex-implement` → `/hybrid-review`, never push to `main`.
