### MAC1 — macOS window verification: build the probe, walk C1-C17, report deltas

**Owner role:** Mac owner — **unnamed, and the machine's architecture NOT VERIFIED.** `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:12`, `:43` and `:61` assign this work to "Mac teammates" on "their Macs" and give them a seven-item day-one list, so **that they exist is Teddy's standing answer, not an open question**; what is unverified is which individual takes it and whether the Mac is Apple Silicon or Intel (`TB:244`, and `TH:49` makes recording that an explicit deliverable). The older, wider claim is kept as provenance only: `TB:199` "no report establishes that the machine exists", and Teddy's session handoff at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/.claude/handoff.md:48` — "`teddy-mbp` in build.py is sample data; no Mac is known" — both of which predate `TEAM-HANDOFF.md`. **Size:** M — one sitting, not one sprint, and expect at least one round trip. **Start today:** yes for step 1, *once Teddy names who on the Mac side takes it* (decision 3, re-scoped) **and once Teddy has published the five probe files** (§2.1).

**Goal and why it matters.** The one job in the M5 decomposition **no other machine can do**. `tauri-driver` is Windows and Linux only; macOS has no WKWebView driver tool, and the documented alternative (`@wdio/tauri-service`) points at a CrabNebula fork **requiring a paid API key** for macOS. **There is no automation. This is seventeen rows of looking.**

**Inputs to open on day one — and this package deliberately points at no document the Mac owner cannot open.** **Primary transport: the five probe files, attached to this ask by Teddy** — `Cargo.toml`, `build.rs`, `lib.rs`, `tauri.conf.json`, `capabilities.default.json`, ~4 KB in total, and step 1 needs nothing else from him. That is the primary route precisely so **step 1 depends on nothing but Teddy's publish act**, and not on another owner's package existing. **Preferred where it exists: `git clone <CI1's scratch public repo — URL to be filled in at hand-off time: ________>`**, which carries the complete scaffolded app CI1 committed (scaffold + the five probe files + generated `icons/` + a committed lockfile) — cloning that is better than scaffolding your own, because it makes your window and CI's bundle the same artifact. **If that repo does not exist yet, use the attachment route and the fallback recipe in step 1.** The files originate at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/probe/`, inside the design folder on Teddy's box — §2.1 makes publishing them his act; **never** `~/.cache/tauri-build-probe`, which is a path on his machine. The seventeen rows, the may/may-not-change rule and the acceptance statement are **reproduced verbatim below** rather than cited, because `TB` (= `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-tauri-build.md`) is not in any repo and a package whose entire contract is a pointer is not a handoff. For step 2 only: `HANDOFF-M5.md` from CI2b.

**Contract.** K6 (§3.4) — the seventeen rows, reproduced here in full from `TB:175-193` so step 1 is self-contained. (CI2a's `HANDOFF-M5.md` ships this same blank table; it is pulled forward here so a Mac owner handed only this section can start.)

**The table is split into two blocks, because six rows are unanswerable against the probe.** The probe window is a scaffolded app with a **stub `index.html` and no Terum chrome at all** — no top bar, no Terum mark, no search field, no bell, no `data-tauri-drag-region`, no three-mode header component. Six rows ask about exactly those, and an earlier draft sent the Mac owner to discover that mid-walk.

**Block 1 — answerable against the probe today (eleven rows). This block plus the step-1 deliverable IS a complete step 1.**

| # | Check | Pass condition | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | macOS window has `titleBarStyle: "Overlay"` **and** `decorations: true` | both present; not `decorations: false` | |
| C2 | `trafficLightPosition` is set in **logical** px | `{ x: 16, y: 14 }` puts the group's top-left where the drawing does. **`y = 14` is authoritative, and the reason is arithmetic rather than preference:** the field addresses the **group's top-left**, while the drawing centres the lights on **y = 20 in a 40 px bar** with **12 px** dots — `TOPBAR_H = 40` (`build.py:173`), the 12 px dot (`:213`), the 8 px gap and 16 px left inset (`:223`); `TB:171` derives the same numbers off the same lines ("12 px dots …, 8 px gap, 16 px left inset, centres at x 22 / 42 / 62 and **y 20** in a 40 px bar"). So top-left `y` = 20 − 12/2 = **14**, which is what `TB:178` records. **`18` matches no drawn value: it is the preserved probe file's own number** (`.review/2026-09-07-tauri-build/probe/tauri.conf.json` ships `"trafficLightPosition": { "x": 16, "y": 18 }`). **What the owner does:** set `y` to 14 before measuring — step 1 authorizes that one edit — and record in the delta table which value the config actually carried. A 4 px delta against the probe's own 18 is a config artifact, not a macOS finding | |
| C3 | Lights land at the drawn inset | leftmost light's left edge 16 px from the window's left; group centred on y = 20 px in a 40 px bar | |
| C4 | Light diameter vs the drawing | drawn dots are 12 px; **measure** the OS-drawn ones and record the delta (native size NOT VERIFIED) | *measurement, not pass/fail* |
| C5 | Light spacing vs the drawing | drawn pitch is 20 px (12 + 8); **measure** the OS pitch, record the delta | *measurement, not pass/fail* |
| C8 | Hidden title | `hiddenTitle: true`; the design draws no window title anywhere | |
| C11 | Double-click-to-zoom | double-clicking the bar background zooms/unzooms; under `Overlay` plus a custom drag region this is hand-implemented via `startDragging()`. *Against the stub, this reduces to: does the OS zoom gesture reach an `Overlay` bar at all* | |
| C12 | Dragging while unfocused | click-drag on an unfocused window still moves it, or the limitation is accepted and written down | |
| C14 | Full-screen | entering full screen keeps the lights positioned and leaves no gap; leaving restores | *observation task, NOT VERIFIED* |
| C15 | Light and dark theme | both render the same hard-coded light fills against the theme's `chrome` background; check contrast in Light | |
| C17 | Undecorated resize (Windows/Linux leg) | resizing an undecorated window is smooth; 2.11.4 shipped resize fixes for undecorated windows, so test it | |

**Block 2 — needs the real app's top bar, and is therefore POST-PUSH (six rows).** Mark them exactly that: **`post-push — no real-app window exists`**, not blank and not failed. **Why it is post-push and not merely "step 2":** the three-mode header is M1's component inside `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, which is local-only with no remote until Teddy creates the public repo and pushes (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:14`, `:61`, `:79`); and there is no `src-tauri/` anywhere to build a native window from, because `BRIEF.md:15` keeps it out of the loop ("src-tauri is NOT created tonight") and `TH:79` calls the post-push M5 shell "empty by design". So the chain is: **Teddy pushes → J1 creates `src-tauri/` → CI2b cuts a Release → these six become answerable.** Under decision 3's default there is no Mac owner and under decision 18's default M5 sits at position five behind M2-M4, so **they may not be answered in phase 1 at all.**

**What the Mac owner does instead, so the six are not simply parked.** After the push and **before** any `src-tauri/` exists, the app runs in a browser: `git clone <terum-skills-app> && npm install && npm run dev` on the Mac, at 1440 × 900. That answers the **geometry** halves against the real component rather than a stub — **C6** (the Terum mark's 24 × 24 box beginning at x = 76, nothing painting left of it), **C7**'s second half (does the row still centre the 480 px search field), **C13** (the 240 + 480 + 240 = 960 px clusters not overlapping at minimum width) — and lets **C16** be checked in its **browser/mock** mode (the cosmetic drawn lights), which is one of that row's three modes. **Record those as "browser-verified, native pending", never as C1-C17 verdicts**: a browser window has no OS-drawn lights, no `Overlay` bar and no window manager, so the two rows that are about the *native* surface — **C9** (dragging the bar background) and **C10** (children not swallowing the drag region) — plus C16's macOS mode and the native half of C7's bar height **cannot** be answered that way and stay post-push. **And a browser render on a Mac is still not a fidelity oracle** (`TH:61`): this is a geometry look, not a diff, and no `.shots` comparison may be run from it.

| # | Check | Pass condition | Why it cannot be answered against the probe |
|---|---|---|---|
| C6 | Nothing collides with the lights | the Terum mark's 24×24 box begins at x = 76 px; nothing paints left of it on macOS | there is no Terum mark in a stub `index.html` |
| C7 | Overlay bar height vs the drawn bar | drawn bar is 40 px; `Overlay` has OS-dependent height variations, so record the actual height and whether the row still centres the search field | the *height* half is answerable against the probe and may be recorded in block 1; "still centres the search field" needs a search field |
| C9 | Drag region works on the bar background | dragging the empty area of the bar moves the window | there is no `data-tauri-drag-region` in a stub `index.html` |
| C10 | Drag region does not silently fail on children | back/forward, the 480 px search field and the bell are each explicitly drag-enabled or explicitly interactive; no dead element | none of those children exist |
| C13 | Min size | `minWidth`/`minHeight` set; at minimum the 240 + 480 + 240 = 960 px clusters do not overlap | the *keys* are set in the probe and can be confirmed; the *clusters* do not exist to overlap |
| C16 | Three-mode header component | macOS OS lights plus reserved gutter; Windows/Linux drawn controls with `decorations: false`; browser/mock the cosmetic drawn lights | the component is M1's and is not in the probe |

**The may / may-not-change rule, verbatim from `TB:169`.** *May:* `tauri.conf.json` window values (`trafficLightPosition` x and y, `minWidth`/`minHeight`, `hiddenTitle`), and the placement of `data-tauri-drag-region` on the top bar. *May not, without coming back:* `titleBarStyle` or `decorations`, because C1 is the whole point, and anything under `src/` outside the seam directory. **This plan narrows the "may" half to report-only — see *Must not touch* below**, which is the change that makes the package clean.

**The acceptance statement, verbatim from `TB:195`.** "**Acceptance: unset, and it is M1's to define.** No source in this evidence set states an acceptance tolerance for a native macOS window." There is no number to hit; see decision 6.

**The geometry behind the rows was re-derived against the frozen `build.py` rather than trusted:** 12 px dots with a 0.5 px inset ring (`build.py:213`), 8 px gap and 16 px left inset (`:223`), a 40 px bar with 240 px side clusters (`:173`), the Terum mark's 24×24 box beginning at x = 76 (`:225`; 16 + (12·3 + 8·2) + 8 = 76), centres at x 22/42/62 and y 20, natural minimum width 960 (240 + 480 + 240). **`TB:171` already records that TF §6's cites of `build.py:204` and `:216` are an eight-line drift and stale** — `def traffic_light` is at `build.py:212` and the red/yellow/green triple at `:224`.

**The row that is the whole point.** **C1 is `titleBarStyle: "Overlay"` *plus* `decorations: true`.** `frontend-stack.md` §8 prescribes the **opposite** (`decorations: false`), and that configuration silently loses the design's lights on macOS. The field docs are unambiguous: "The position of the window controls on macOS. **Requires `titleBarStyle: Overlay` and `decorations: true`.**" **NOT VERIFIED, and this is why the row exists** (`TB:241` item 1): that requirement is a *runtime* claim, and only the config *keys* were validated at check time on the Linux box. C1 is the observation that settles it — not a config audit.

**Two steps, deliberately ordered so step 1 needs nothing from anyone.**

1. **Open a window at all.**

   **Preferred, and the fastest path: clone CI1's scratch repo** (URL blank above) and run `npm install && npm run tauri dev`. CI1 commits the **complete scaffolded app** — scaffold + the five probe files + generated `icons/` + a committed lockfile, with `trafficLightPosition.y` already set to 14 — so you build the same artifact CI builds and you report the Tauri and Rust versions the repo already pins, rather than whatever your own scaffold happens to pick. **This is what makes your C1-C17 verdict describe the same window as CI's bundle**, which is the ambiguity C1 exists to settle.

   **Fallback, if CI1's repo does not exist** (decision 4 vetoed toward the CLI repo, or decision 19 leaving the CI owner unstaffed): scaffold it yourself. **The preserved probe is a `cargo check` fixture, not a runnable app** — it has no `package.json`, no `../dist`, no `src/main.rs` bin entry (its `Cargo.toml` declares `[lib]` only) and no `icons/`, while its `tauri.conf.json` declares `beforeDevCommand: "npm run dev"` and `bundle.targets: "all"` with five icon paths. So: `xcode-select --install`; the rustup one-liner; `cargo install tauri-cli`; **`npm create tauri-app` to scaffold**, then **overwrite** `src-tauri/{Cargo.toml, build.rs, src/lib.rs, tauri.conf.json, capabilities/default.json}` with the five probe files, `npx tauri icon` to generate the icon set, and **keep the scaffold's `package.json`, its stub `index.html` and its `src-tauri/src/main.rs`** — without a bin entry and a frontend there is nothing for a window to show. Then `npm install && npm run tauri dev`. These files **already type-check for `aarch64-apple-darwin` from Linux**; the thing Linux cannot do is open a real window.

   **One authorized edit, and it is the only exception to *report a delta, never edit*: the preserved probe ships `"trafficLightPosition": { "x": 16, "y": 18 }`, and C2's drawn value is `{ x: 16, y: 14 }` (`TB:178`). Set `y` to 14 before walking C2, and record in the delta table which value the config carried.** Without this the sitting produces a guaranteed 4 px "delta" that is an artifact of the probe file rather than of macOS — and the delta report cannot distinguish the two, which is exactly the ambiguity this package exists to remove. (CI1's committed scaffold already carries 14, so on the preferred path there is nothing to change.)

   **Deliverable — and this, plus block 1 of the table, is a complete step 1:** a screenshot showing the Overlay title bar and the traffic lights at the drawn positions; **block 1's eleven rows filled**; the Tauri and Rust versions used; and **whether the Mac is Apple Silicon or Intel** — the architecture is NOT VERIFIED (`TB:244` item 4) and CI1's asset matrix depends on it. Returning that is done; block 2 is not owed at step 1. *Caveat, from `TB:167`:* the three setup commands in the fallback are **inferred** from the `macos-latest` runner inventory (Xcode 26.6 + CLT, Rust 1.98 with rustup, Node 24.20), not read off Tauri's macOS prerequisites page, and are **NOT VERIFIED complete**.
2. **Walk block 2's six rows against the real app**, from CI2b's Release tarball — **post-push, and gated on Teddy pushing, then J1 creating `src-tauri/`, then CI2b cutting a Release.** **Under decision 3's default there is no Mac owner and under decision 18's default CI2b sits behind M2-M4, so step 2 may never happen in phase 1 — record the six as `post-push — no real-app window exists` rather than as failures.** **The intermediate pass, when it is available:** once Teddy has pushed, `npm run dev` on the Mac answers C6, C7's centring half, C13 and C16's browser mode against the real component in a browser — mark those **browser-verified, native pending**, and leave C9, C10 and C16's macOS mode for the native window. That pass needs no Rust, no CI and no Release; it needs only the push.

**Two rows are not pass/fail.** C4 (light diameter) and C5 (pitch) are **measure-then-record** — the *native* macOS diameter and pitch were never established from an Apple source (`TB:242` item 2). C14 (full-screen behaviour with `Overlay`) is explicitly an observation task, NOT VERIFIED.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** **Step 1: block 1's eleven rows completed** — pass / fail / cannot-judge per row, C4 and C5 as recorded measurements, C14 as an observation — plus the screenshot, the version note, the Apple-Silicon-vs-Intel answer, and the `trafficLightPosition` value the config carried. **That is the whole of step 1 and it is accepted alone.** Step 2 adds block 2's six rows and is gated on CI2b, not on this owner. **There is no numeric tolerance, and that is a decision not an oversight** — `TB:195` states it: "Acceptance: unset, and it is M1's to define." No source in the evidence set states an acceptance tolerance for a native macOS window, and the only baseline that exists is the browser-side 0-px one, which nothing converts into a gate on a `.app`. See decision 6.

**The "if the window is blank" note, which must ship with the package.** `tauri#15517` — "[macOS 26 Tahoe] tao 0.35.3 panics in `did_finish_launching`, Tauri 2.11.2 GUI window opens blank" — is **open against exactly the 2.11.x line this experiment pinned**, severity on 2.11.5 unknown, and `macos-latest` **is** macOS 26 (`TB:245` item 5). `tauri#15801` (Tahoe AMFI 16 KB page-size rejection, single unconfirmed report, mitigation `--pagesize=4096`) is the second candidate. **The instruction is: report the exact macOS version and stop. Do not debug. A blank window is a finding in itself, and C1-C17 are unanswerable in that state.** Without this note a Mac owner acting from the package alone reads a known upstream bug as a broken build.

**The recurrence cost, which decides E vs B.** Under option E, **every fix iteration is a new bundle the Mac owner re-downloads**, and `TB:243` item 3 records three unknowns that bite on each one: **whether an Open Anyway exception survives a version bump**, whether Open Anyway appears at all for the *damaged* verdict, and that **macOS caches negative Gatekeeper verdicts**. So the five-step Privacy & Security ritual may recur per rebuild rather than once. That is precisely the threshold in decision 2: **if the chrome needs more than about three iterations, B was cheaper.**

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: **a Mac existing at all** (decision 3), its architecture; **Teddy publishing the five probe files** (§2.1) — step 1 has nothing to build without them; **CI1, on the preferred path only** — cloning its scratch repo is how step 1 gets the same artifact CI builds, and §5.3 therefore schedules step 1 for **Day 1 afternoon / Day 2**, after CI1 has created that repo. **Step 1 does not *require* CI1: with the five files attached to the ask (the primary transport above), the fallback scaffold recipe makes step 1 depend on nothing but Teddy's publish act.** For step 2 under option E: **a Release from CI2b carrying a non-empty asset** (CI1 does not supply this — it builds the probe in a scratch repo, never the real app — and CI2b is itself gated on Teddy's J1). Also depends on the accepted risk that `tauri#15517` returns a blank window and the sitting yields only a macOS-version report. Unblocks: M5's chrome verdict, and M1's config set if a delta comes back.

**Must not touch — the condition that makes this package CLEAN.** As written, `TB:169` grants the Mac owner edit rights on `tauri.conf.json` window values (`trafficLightPosition` x/y, `minWidth`/`minHeight`, `hiddenTitle`) and on the `data-tauri-drag-region` placement. **That file is J1's.** Narrowed here to **report a delta, never edit — with exactly one written exception, `trafficLightPosition.y` = 14 in step 1, because the preserved probe ships 18 and C2's drawn target is 14** (`TB:178`); that edit is authorized, is recorded in the delta table, and is the only one. The narrowing is the single change that turns J1 and J4 from one shared task into two handoffs. Also off limits, per `TB:169` itself: `titleBarStyle` and `decorations` (C1 is the whole point), and anything under `src/` outside the seam directory — **which seam directory is §6 decision 16**, defaulting to `src/backend/tauri/`. **This narrowing holds under decision 2 = E only.** Under **B**, MAC1 absorbs J1/J2, owns `tauri.conf.json` outright, "never edit" is false, and its acceptance becomes a green per-target `cargo check` plus the filled C1-C17 table — **re-scope the package before handing it out**, because under B it is a different job with a different gate. **Plus the standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`) — the probe files come to you, you never reach into the design folder for them; *(b)* **do not clone or recreate the app repo — it does not exist outside Teddy's box until he pushes**, and **do not render boards on a Mac: a Mac render is not a valid fidelity oracle** (`TH:61`, verbatim, and `TH:9` for the reason — the shots are Playwright Chromium 1243 Linux renders at 0.2-0.35 % tolerance); *(c)* **no PRs that rename the CLI's printed strings** (`TH:61`; `BRIEF.md:28`) — a copy delta you notice while walking the window is a line for Teddy, not a patch.

**Blocking Teddy decisions, with their defaults.** **2 — E or B for M5; default E**, under which this is a one-hour look and the *Must not touch* narrowing above holds; **under B this package absorbs J1/J2, owns `tauri.conf.json` outright, "never edit" is false, and its acceptance becomes a green per-target `cargo check` plus the filled table — a different job with a different gate.** **3 — does a Mac owner exist; default: no**, in which case this package is *unowned* and C1-C17 stays unanswered. **Read 2 and 3 together: B is only available once 3 is answered yes. Answering B while 3 stands at its default leaves J1, J2, J4 and the whole of M5 with no owner at all — that is not a choice between two plans, it is a choice to have none.** **6 — the C1-C17 acceptance tolerance; default: unset, and defining it is M1's job** (`TB:195`), so C4 and C5 are recorded as measurements rather than verdicts. **16 — the seam path; default `src/backend/tauri/`**, which is the boundary of the "anything under `src/`" clause. Plus the non-decision dependency: **Teddy publishing the five probe files**, without which step 1 has nothing to build.

**Review path.** No repo. A table and a screenshot, returned to Teddy.

### FE1 — Receipt fixture conformance kit + the §12 hard-rule suite

**Owner role:** **eval owner (Ajay)**, who owns `receiptSchema`'s meaning — matching §5.2's lane B. *Fallback if he is unavailable:* the sidecar developer, who consumes the fixtures — **but then SIDE2's fixture dependency becomes internal and its contract test stops being an independent check**, so the fallback costs something and is not a free swap. **Size:** S. **Start today:** yes — it needs no decision from Teddy.

*(An earlier draft invented a sixth "frontend-infrastructure helper" role to hold this one package, in a section that then argued every other candidate for that role fails CLEAN. A role with exactly one job and no other work is a role nobody staffs. It belongs with Ajay.)*

**Goal and why it matters.** The one frontend-side job that is genuinely orphaned, needs no design-folder write, and lives outside every path the loop owns. **`INV:195` asserts a precondition — "The receipt fixtures must be shaped to `receiptSchema` **exactly**, because no real receipt exists yet and the app's first real data will be zod-parsed against it" — and that precondition is currently false.** If it stays false, the mismatch surfaces at M6 as a UI change, which is M6's exit criterion failing — `INV:305`, the M6 row: "`src/backend/tauri/` + the sidecar + the CLI's `exports` map; **the UI does not change**; capability flags flip from the adapter". (**`INV` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-investigation.md`**, an uncommitted file on Teddy's box that **he attaches to the ask** — it is in no repo, verified at the pin. Both sentences it supplies are quoted above, so it is provenance rather than an input.)

**The gap, measured this session by importing the frozen snapshot, not quoted.** `build.DETAIL['receipt']` has keys `abstract, arm, attribution, catalog, cc, coverage, date, eff, efficiency_text, engine, inc_p, incumbent, judge, k, model, per_case, results, run_id, runner, sign_p, timestamp, trigger_text, triggers, when`. **`receiptSchema` (`src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50-87`) requires fifteen fields.** The design supplies **three of them by name** — `run_id`, `attribution`, `triggers` — and **twelve are absent**: `schema_version, skill_id, skill_name, version, verdict, execution_status, expected_rows, scored_rows, comparisons, arm_scores, efficiency, provenance`. Of the three present, **one fails its type**: `triggers.recall` and `.precision` are the **strings** `"1.00"` where the schema demands `z.number().nullable()` (`receipt.ts:67-68`). `run_id = "20260829T221500Z"` does match `RUN_ID_PATTERN` (`:47`). And the design carries **`per_case`**, which `receiptSchema` does not have — precisely the additive ask filed as EVAL1 ruling 4.

**The design side of the mapping, pasted verbatim so the package is self-contained.** Read by importing the frozen snapshot, `build.py` md5 **`43f4d0901f4eb618dc33f692f70a3aa2`**. `build.DETAIL['receipt']` has **24 keys**; here are all of them except the five prose strings, which follow.

```json

 "runner": "ajay", "run_id": "20260829T221500Z", "catalog": 30,
 "incumbent": {"wlt": [8, 5, 5], "version": "3b1c9d4e2f70"},
 "sign_p": "0.057", "inc_p": "0.581",
 "arm": {"candidate": 0.82, "incumbent": 0.74, "baseline": 0.61},

              "recall": "1.00", "precision": "1.00", "misses": []},

         "incumbent": ["10.6", "46 s", "$0.44"],
         "baseline":  ["12.1", "53 s", "$0.51"]},
 "attribution": "wins on execution checks; judge ties on style cases",
 "model": "sonnet", "judge": "sonnet", "cc": "2.34.0", "k": 3, "engine": "0.1.0",
 "per_case": [["stale-env-vars",      1.00, 0.78, [2, 1, 0]],
              ["migration-drift",     0.89, 0.44, [3, 0, 0]],
              ["secret-scan-gate",    0.83, 0.67, [1, 0, 2]],
              ["rollback-plan",       0.78, 0.44, [3, 0, 0]],
              ["healthcheck-wiring",  0.75, 0.67, [1, 1, 1]],
              ["dns-cutover-order",   0.67, 0.67, [1, 1, 1]]]}
```

**The five prose strings at `build.py:867-878`, verbatim** (`P_BASE = 0.057`, `P_INC = 0.581` already substituted, as the f-strings do):

- **`abstract`** — "Six execution cases were run at k = 3 repetitions across three arms (baseline, candidate, incumbent), with trigger selection measured against the team catalog of 30 skills. The candidate outperformed baseline in 11 of 18 rounds (net lift +0.44, exact two-sided sign test p = 0.057) and routed correctly on all should-trigger prompts with no false fires. Verdict: PASS."
- **`results`** — "Mean deterministic-check pass rate was 0.82 for the candidate, 0.74 for the incumbent, and 0.61 for baseline (Fig. 1). Against baseline the candidate won 11, lost 3, and tied 4 of 18 rounds (net lift +0.44, p = 0.057; 12 rounds settled by checks, 2 by judge). Against the incumbent the record was 8–5–5 (net lift +0.17, p = 0.581) (Fig. 2). Per-case separations were largest for migration-drift and rollback-plan; dns-cutover-order showed none (Table 1)."
- **`trigger_text`** — "Selecting from the team catalog of 30 skills, the agent chose the candidate on 6 of 6 should-trigger prompts (recall 1.00) and on 0 of 5 near-miss distractors (precision 1.00)."
- **`efficiency_text`** — "The candidate was cheaper and faster than both comparators on every measure (Table 2)."
- **`coverage`** — "All 36 of 36 rounds scored; all 6 hygiene screens passed. The sign test is informational at k = 3 and did not gate the verdict. Raw transcripts and sandboxes remain on ajay's machine under run 20260829T221500Z; all committed content passed secret redaction."

**Inputs to open on day one.** A checkout at the pin (`git clone https://github.com/ryanliu-terum/terum-skills && git checkout b5c0507`), then `src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:47-93`; `src/lib/readme.ts:198, :224`; `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md` §12 `:495-498`, §5.4 `:253-254`; **the design data pasted in full below**; EVAL1 rulings 3 and 4. **The design folder is not an input and cannot be** — §7 rules it untransportable (not a git repo, one copy on Teddy's box, an md5 that goes stale the same day), and this package's *must not touch* forbids it. **This package needs the data, not the folder, and pasting the data is the only transport §7 permits.**

**Contract.** K5 (§3.3) — `receiptSchema` `receipt.ts:50-87` (fifteen required fields, `environment_skips` optional at `:65`, `.passthrough()` at `:87`); `RUN_ID_PATTERN` `:47`; the 40-char lowercase tree-hash `VERSION_PATTERN` at `:48` (**note: not exported** — mirror the regex, do not import it); §12's four hard rules at `:495-498`; the shipped partial-render convention at `src/lib/readme.ts:198` — `` `${verdict} — partial (${scored}/${expected} scored)` `` — **copy it, do not invent one**; and §12:491's **12**-char version, **not** `src/lib/readme.ts:224`'s `shortHash`, which truncates to 8 for the README table. Different surfaces, not a conflict — but the card must use 12.

**Deliverables** — a standalone `receipt-kit/` directory, dropped in after Teddy pushes; **never written into `src/fixtures/`**, which the loop owns.

**Where `receipt-kit/` lives before the push, and how `receiptSchema` is obtained — stated because neither was named, and the package is graded on the second.** **Home: the sidecar's own repo, under decision 5's default**, in a top-level `receipt-kit/` beside SIDE0's `contract/` and SIDE1's `protocol/` — the same answer decision 5 already gives those two, and it means this package has a `package.json`, a vitest config and a review path from day one instead of being a directory with no repo. *(If Teddy would rather it not sit in the sidecar repo, its own scratch repo works identically; what does not work is "no repo", which is what an earlier draft's "dropped in after Teddy pushes" amounted to.)* **Recipe, the same one SIDE0 uses:** a scratch npm project with vitest; `npm install && npm run build` in the pinned checkout (`git clone https://github.com/ryanliu-terum/terum-skills && git checkout b5c0507`), then `npm pack` it and install the tarball. **`receiptSchema` is reachable only by deep import — `terum-skills/dist/lib/evals/receipt.js` — and it is untyped until CLI1 lands**, because `package.json` at the pin has **no `exports`, no `main`, no `types`** and `tsconfig.build.json` has **no `declaration`**, so no `.d.ts` is emitted. `package.json`'s `files` array at `:17-22` is `["dist","LICENSE","NOTICE","README.md"]`, so the whole `dist` tree ships and the deep path resolves. **Mirror the `Receipt` type locally from `receipt.ts:88`; do not wait for CLI1** — this package is on nobody's critical path and blocking it on Ryan's PR would put it there.
1. A **field-by-field mapping table**: design key → schema field → conversion, with the twelve absent fields filled with defensible values and the two string→number coercions named.
2. `receipt-kit/fixtures/*.json` — at least a complete PASS receipt, a `partial` one, and the **full nullability sweep** the mock must exercise, all of which are reachable on real data: `triggers` null · `triggers.recall`/`.precision` null · every `arm_scores` value null · every `efficiency.*.turns`/`duration_ms`/`cost_usd` null · `environment_skips` **absent** (optional at `:65`) · `provenance.arm_skill_lists[arm]` null · `comparisons['candidate-vs-incumbent']` absent for a new skill · **`provenance.engine_commit === 'unknown'`**, which is what every npm-installed CLI produces (`eval.ts:212, :215` — the 12-hex short commit comes only from a source checkout).
3. `receipt-kit/hard-rules.ts` **and** `receipt-kit/hard-rules.spec.ts` — **exported pure predicates plus their unit tests, not UI assertions.** §12's four rules, verbatim at `eval-engine.md:495-498`, all constrain *rendering*: "no skill list is ever sorted or ranked by any receipt number; no lift-style decimal appears at card level; a skill without a receipt shows '—' (phase-1 D22 behavior); numbers from different `model`/`cc_version` never appear in one comparison surface". **This package has no card, no list and no app to assert them against** — its own acceptance says "no browser, no design folder and no app" — so what it can actually ship on day one, and does, is the enforceable core of each rule as a function:
   - `assertNoRankByReceiptNumber(order, receipts)` — throws if the given display order correlates with any receipt number (`:495`);
   - `cardFields(receipt | null)` — returns `'—'` for every slot when the receipt is null (`:496-497`), and never emits a lift-style decimal at card level (`:495-496`);
   - `cardVersion(receipt)` — `receipt.version.slice(0, 12)`, per §12:491's 12 chars, **not** `src/lib/readme.ts:224`'s 8-char `shortHash`;
   - `assertSameProvenance(receipts)` — rejects a set mixing `model` or `cc_version` (`:497-498`, §5.4 `:253-254`);
   - `partialLabel(verdict, scored, expected)` — the shipped convention at `src/lib/readme.ts:198`, copied not invented.

   Each predicate carries the §12 line it enforces in a comment. **The app imports these and calls them inside its own render tests at M4/M6** — that is where "no lift decimal appears at card level" becomes checkable. FE1 ships the predicates and the fixtures; the UI assertions are the app's, and belong in `GAPS.md`/M4 rather than here.
4. **`per_case` carried as a `.passthrough()` extra** on at least one fixture, so the block round-trips without the schema knowing about it — the phase-1 shape while EVAL1 ruling 4 is outstanding and the affordance is flag-gated.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** `receiptSchema.safeParse(fixture).success === true` for every fixture — with `receiptSchema` obtained by the deep-import recipe above, **not by a bare `import { receiptSchema } from 'terum-skills'`, which does not resolve at the pin** — and `vitest run receipt-kit/` green. Both run anywhere, with no browser, no design folder and no app.

**Depends on / unblocks.** Depends on: nothing technical (the deep import works today). Unblocks: **SIDE2**'s contract tests (which assert every `receipts` return matches these fixtures field for field), and the app's first real-data render.

**Must not touch.** `build.py` and the whole design folder; `src/fixtures/**`, `tools/**` (including `tools/export-design.py`), `src/styles/tokens.css`, `tests/unit/fixtures/**`, `playwright.config.ts`, `FIDELITY.md`, `MASKS.md` — **all loop-owned, and all written by Codex under `BRIEF.md:18`, not by a person**; the CLI repo. **Plus the standing rules:** *(a)* **while the `/goal` loop runs, nothing writes `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app`, `~/Projects/terum-skills-app-codex` or `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on Teddy's box** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` = `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/TEAM-HANDOFF.md`) — which is why this package's home is the sidecar's own repo and its deliverable is a standalone `receipt-kit/`, merged only after the push; *(b)* **the public `terum-skills-app` does not exist until Teddy pushes** (`TH:14`, `:61`), so `receipt-kit/` is never "dropped into" a clone that is not there yet; *(c)* **no printed string changes** (`BRIEF.md:28`; `TH:61`) — the five prose strings pasted above are **design data this package maps**, never copy it edits, and `build.py` regeneration is decision 13's non-default answer and Teddy's.

**Blocking Teddy decisions, with their defaults.** **13 — regenerate the design's receipt, or map at the boundary; map at the boundary is now more than the default — the alternative is foreclosed.** `BRIEF.md:60` (the 2026-09-08 03:50 UTC speed directive, final, never re-ask) closes the design side: "No further design patches, renders, canvas re-reads, or design-side re-verification." Regenerating the design's receipt is a `build.py` edit, so it cannot happen while that stands, and **this package's shape is settled rather than contingent.** It ceases to exist only if Teddy re-opens the design folder, which is his call and nobody else's. **4 — repo placement for the app; default: public sibling `terum-skills-app`**; under the non-default answer (the app inside the CLI repo) **this package's merge path becomes spec delta → `/codex-spec` → `/codex-implement` → `/hybrid-review` in Ryan's repo, never push to `main`, and the merge needs Ryan** — re-scope before handing it out. **5 — does the sidecar live in its own repo; default: its own repo**, which is where `receipt-kit/` sits before the push (see *Deliverables*). Integration timing is a package-internal default: **post-loop**, delivered as a standalone directory and merged after the push, so nothing collides.

**Review path.** The sidecar repo (decision 5's default) until Teddy pushes; then an ordinary PR against `terum-skills-app` — or Ryan's pipeline under decision 4's non-default answer.

**One standing constraint on all of it:** **nothing frontend-internal is handed out until Teddy pushes `terum-skills-app`** — standing rule 5, and it has two independent reasons rather than one. **(1) The repo is not reachable.** It is local-only with no remote (`BRIEF.md:43`; measured: 0 remotes) and `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:61` tells teammates not to clone or recreate it, so before the push an app-repo "deliverable" is a directory in a tarball and its integration is a merge nobody has scheduled. **(2) The repo is write-locked and single-author.** Standing rule 2 (`TH:17`) bars a second writer, and `BRIEF.md:18` makes Codex the author of every line of app code with Claude specifying and verifying — so "help with a screen" is not slow, it is *impossible to accept*: there is no branch to open it on and no gate a second author could run. **After the push both reasons lapse at once**, every such package becomes an ordinary PR against a real repo with a real branch, the whole conflict analysis evaporates, and §7.2's three post-loop packages become available. The push is therefore worth treating as a scheduled event rather than a formality.

## 6. Teddy's decisions, in the order they block work

Every row states the default that applies **if he says nothing**. Rows 1-4 block a lane; the rest shape a package. **This table is the decision index — if a call is Teddy's, it has a row here.** Three that earlier drafts carried only in prose (the seam path, `setup` step 8, the M5 build order) are numbered **16, 17 and 18**. ### 6.1 Smaller calls carried inside their own packages, deliberately not escalated

So the table above stays the calls only Teddy can make. **Two items an earlier draft parked here have been promoted out of it, because neither was a package-internal call:** the `gh auth login` behaviour is **decision 21** (it changes a drawn affordance, and §2.1 keeps every design change), and the CLI1 authorship question is **§7.1 item 6**, narrowed. **`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55` already assigns the drafting**: "Draft the four packaging asks against Ryan's repo as one PR on `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` … Do not merge; Ryan reviews." So "a teammate drafts the PR" is not a default this document invents — it is Teddy's standing instruction, and CLI1 is stated accordingly: **drafted by a teammate per `TH:55`, reviewed and merged by Ryan.** What remains open, and is genuinely **Ryan's answer, not a default anyone here can take**, is whether a non-Ryan author may open a PR in that repo at all, given `teniroo` is `admin: false`, branch protection is NOT VERIFIED, and some approvals are Ryan-only; **it rides the decision-1 ask.**

What genuinely remains package-internal: whether the sidecar string-matches the nine decline strings rather than waiting for a typed `Result` (**yes, string-match — after the `execute.ts:30-32` mapping**, SIDE2); and FE1's integration timing (**post-loop**, a standalone directory in the sidecar repo, merged after the push).

## 7. What is NOT handed off, and why

Each row fails a named clause of the CLEAN bar in §2.2. The last group is the one this plan adds: drawn affordances for which **no contract exists at all**, listed with evidence so they are not re-proposed next week.

| Item | Which clause it fails |
|---|---|
| **The seam's inward half as a package** — `types.ts` + `Backend.ts` + `mock/` + `prompter.ts` + `index.ts`, **except one arm.** `src/backend/index.ts` stays the loop's **except the single `case 'tauri':` arm of `pickBackend()` (`INV:90`), which SIDE2 adds as a one-line PR after the re-home** — without it M6's exit criterion ("capability flags flip from the adapter", `INV:305`) is unobservable, and with it unassigned `src/backend/` would have two writers and no owner for the one edit M6 is graded on | *One owner* and *never edits a file another package edits*. Written by the loop's S1a/S1b inside a single-writer repo, and **co-designed with the mock and the screens** — the mock is where every capability-flagged affordance lives, so its shape follows from Teddy's board decisions rather than from a handoff. Its outward half **is** separable and is SIDE1 |
| **`tools/export-design.py`, `src/fixtures/`, `src/styles/tokens.css`, the ten ported data gates** | Three clauses at once: writes files the loop writes; needs the design folder, which is **not a git repo** and lives as one copy on Teddy's box (the only transport is a copy at an md5 that goes stale the same day — **four values are now on the record**: `b3aea6df…` at prep, which is what `GAPS-seed.md:3` and `HO:42` cite; `43f4d090…`, this document's frozen snapshot; `08ba1c22…` after `patch_z2.py` landed the Discord invite, recorded at `BRIEF:56` and `HO:57`; and **`e98f519e…` measured live for the 2026-09-08 revision**, 4,658 lines, `canvas.json` `c1d5245a…`, 99 artboards, 99 shots. | **A typed decline on `Result`; exporting the batched `skillTrees` reader** | **Approvals on Ryan's core files, already queued in the §9.2 batch — not tasks.** The typed decline changes `src/lib/result.ts:1-6` plus nine producers plus every `!ok` consumer. `skillTrees` is one `export` keyword, but it sits at `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:285` — the file that owns `safeWrite` and the repo's most invariant-heavy module. Folding either into CLI1 would mix an approved no-behaviour-change edit with an unapproved API-surface change and jeopardise that PR. The sidecar's meanwhile-workaround is the nine-string match |
| **A JSON-lines prompter as a package separate from CLI1** | It needs a second `bin` entry (`src/index.ts:43` hard-codes `terminalPrompter`) **and** `eslint.config.js:16`'s `ignores` exemption array widened — and that array is the mechanical enforcement of the library-first rule, chosen as a lint rule precisely because non-Claude agents do not run the repo's hooks. Widening it is a spec-level act. **In-process import avoids it entirely**, which is the strongest practical argument for option O2 |
| **Anything named `ui`** | Three existing named vitest assertions (`setup.test.ts:246, :402, :519`) plus five spec lines plus D21. One indivisible Ryan act, already deferred at `INV:278` |
| **`per_case` as an independent CLI task** | *One owner*: its shape is a §5.3 decision (Ajay) and its implementation is CLI work (Ryan) — two owners in sequence is a shared task. And `BRIEF:6` puts per-case eval tables behind a capability flag, so phase 1 does not need it. It is **EVAL1 ruling 4**, and FE1 carries it as a `receiptSchema` passthrough extra meanwhile |
| **The M1 config set; the seam path** | Spans a frontend file **and** a Rust-side file, so splitting it puts two owners in the same two files. §2.1 |
| **J5 — Developer ID signing, notarization, distribution** | $99/yr, a Mac, and the Account Holder: a purchase plus a distribution decision, not a coding task. **Deferred** (decision 11). Windows signing deferred too — unsigned is two clicks. Named here explicitly because two of the three partitions dropped it silently |
| **The Windows second look** | Option E puts it on Teddy's own Windows-on-ARM laptop, with no Rust and no MSVC ARM64 toolset, addable only at a UAC prompt he alone can answer. §2.1 |
| **Uncosted rows — the category, which no longer has members** | **The principle stands: nobody can own a row that has not been costed, and an id without a change list is not scope Ryan can accept, it is a chase.** What has changed is that the category is now empty. When this row was written, of 196 rows in 28 batches **8 were reconciled** (`MC-S1, TJ-O1, TJ-O2, RM-S1, RM-O1, CP-O2, CP-O3, CP-O4`), 135 were one coster's unrefuted opinion, and 53 came from six batches that had halted at the pin gate with `rows_found: 0`. **`GC` finished that work: 196 rows costed, 0 uncosted**, each refuted by two adversarial lenses and reconciled, with 30 verdicts flipped away from the coster (`GC` §7). The four ids CLI2 had carried as bare labels — **`IB-06`, `CP-37`, `CP-40`** — now have change lists in **`GC` §2** and re-enter CLI2 **by citation**, exactly on the condition this row named; **`CP-39` does not re-enter**, because `GC` §4 costs it **MEDIUM** and CLI2 is the SIMPLE delta. **The rule that produced the strike is kept deliberately**: a row enters a package only with a target `file:line` at the pin, the field or string added, and the test that must move — and now the place that holds all three is `GC`, not a second copy here |
| **Drawn affordances with no contract anywhere** — a real progress panel or a cancel for a running eval; total run cost or duration; the "eval finished" Inbox kind and the activity feed; "Hygiene last ran 12 days ago" | **No producer exists, so there is nothing to hand over.** Verified this session: `eval.ts:142` threads only `io.print(line)` — there is no progress emitter; `EvalResult` (`eval.ts:43-51`) records no total cost or duration; **nothing persists a hygiene run**; and `grep -rln "eventLog\|activity_log\|appendEvent" src --include=*.ts` returns **nothing** — there is no event log anywhere in `src/`. Each of these is a `GAPS.md` line or a capability flag, not a package |

### 7.1 The shared tasks that remain shared

**Six** items are real work that must happen and cannot be a single-owner package. Calling them handoffs would be the mistake. Items 4, 5 and 6 were inside packages in earlier drafts and are here because the evidence moved them: EVAL1 cannot amend Ryan's ledger, EVAL1 cannot merge Ryan's §9, and nobody but Ryan can say who may open a PR in his repo.

1. **The M1 config set and the seam-path choice.** Two files, two owners, one decision. Teddy takes it as part of M1.
2. **`per_case` end to end.** Ajay rules (EVAL1 ruling 4), then Ryan implements, then the frontend renders it behind a flag. Three roles in sequence. Phase 1 does not need it; do not start it.
3. **The typed decline on `Result`.** Ryan approves the type change, then nine call sites move, then the sidecar drops its string-match. It is on his batch already. The sidecar's meanwhile-behaviour is defined (§6.1) so nothing waits.
4. **The three ledger rows in `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md`: D27, D22 and D21.** Ajay rules on the data question, Ryan writes the rows — **two owners in sequence, which is a shared task and not a package**, so they came out of EVAL1. The file is Ryan's: `**Author:** Ryan Liu` at `:5`, and his §9.2 ask row already moves D21 (`:134`) in it. The three: **D27** (`:148`, PROPOSED) contradicts §12:480 on whether the app runs evals — **Ajay** writes the data rule into §12 (that half is EVAL1 ruling 2 and is his alone), **Ryan** amends D27 to point at it; **D22** (`:135`) still says the eval column is `"—" until phase 3` while `src/lib/readme.ts:137` fills it today — Ajay supplies the finding, Ryan writes the correction; **D21** (`:134`) is T1 on Ryan's ask row already. All three land in one ledger edit on Ryan's side, so this costs one trip, not three.
5. **The one §9 sentence that makes the structured hygiene payload contractual — CLI4 item 1's only blocker.** **Ajay drafts it, Ryan approves and merges it**, because **§9 is Ryan's section**: `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:3` records "rev 12 = §9 predicates frozen (audit finding 19, applied on Ryan's continue)" and "rev 16 = HYG6 size cap demoted to a non-blocking warning (Ryan, 2026-09-07)", and the §9 API block at `:420-426` is stamped "(rev 16 — pure, per §3 and ME1)". An earlier draft assigned it to Ajay alone with acceptance "Ajay alone, today" and "merges on Ajay's own approval" — **two owners in sequence, exactly the defect D27/D22 were moved out for**, plus an authorization (may Ajay merge a spec section Ryan froze?) that Teddy has not given and no cited line supports. **Fold it into the same trip as item 4**, since both are Ryan's signature on spec text.
6. **Whether a non-Ryan author may open a PR in `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` at all.** **This half is Ryan's answer, not a default this plan can take** — `teniroo` is `admin: false`, branch protection and required reviews are **NOT VERIFIED** (unreadable for the same reason), and some approvals are Ryan-only. **What is NOT open is who drafts CLI1**: `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:55` is the standing assignment — "Draft the four packaging asks against Ryan's repo as one PR on `ryanliu-terum/terum-skills` … All four are non-breaking. **Do not merge; Ryan reviews.**" An earlier revision of this item asserted that "nothing cited supports" a teammate opening the PR and concluded that CLI1 through CLI4 are wholly Ryan's; **both halves of that were wrong** — `TH:55` supports it verbatim, and asserting otherwise re-assigned a `TH` item out of the teammates' hands, which this document never does. **CLI1 is drafted by a teammate per `TH:55`, reviewed and merged by Ryan.** The authorization question **rides the decision-1 ask** — one extra line in Ryan's row — rather than being assumed either way.

### 7.2 Post-loop packages: the three things a frontend helper may take AFTER the loop

**Everything §7 withholds is withheld for the duration of the single-writer window, and three of those items stop being unsplittable the moment it closes.** They are written as packages here so that "not now" does not get read as "never", and so the person who asks "what can I do on the frontend?" gets an answer with a date on it rather than a refusal.

**The gate all three sit behind is one event with two halves:** the `/goal` loop ends, **and** Teddy creates the public `terum-skills-app`, adds `origin` and pushes `main` (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:14`, `:79`; `BRIEF.md:43`). Before both halves, standing rules 2 and 5 apply and **none of these may start** — there is no repo to clone and the local one is write-locked. `TH:79` is the line that anticipates them: after the push "the Mac teammates work in it … On a Mac, run `npm run check` minus `e2e:fidelity` (typecheck, lint, vitest, `e2e:routes`); **the fidelity gate stays Linux-bound until Gate B baselines are committed**."

**What none of them is.** Not a screen, not a board, not the scaffold, not `tools/export-design.py`, not `src/fixtures/`, not `src/styles/tokens.css`, not the Gate A harness, not `FIDELITY.md` or `MASKS.md`, and not a design decision. Those stay Teddy's under §2.1 and §7 permanently, not merely for the run — Gate A's oracle is Linux-rendered `.shots` and a lock is a design judgement. **These three add a second, portable check beside Gate A; they never touch Gate A.**

---

#### POST1 — Gate B baselines on the other platforms

**Owner role:** any teammate with a Mac or a Windows box — **the Mac owner is the natural holder** (they are already the only person on that platform). **Size:** M. **Start:** after the loop ends **and** Teddy pushes. **Maps to `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:79`.**

**Goal and why it matters.** `BRIEF.md:49` is explicit that "**Gate B (committed app baselines) is not required tonight**", and `TH:79` names the consequence: until Gate B baselines exist, **the fidelity gate is Linux-bound** and a Mac or Windows contributor can run only `npm run check` minus `e2e:fidelity`. That is a real hole — it means nobody but Teddy can catch a visual regression — and it is the one frontend job whose acceptance a non-Linux owner can genuinely run alone, because a Gate B baseline is **the app's own render on that platform**, not a comparison against the design's `.shots`.

**Contract.** The app's `playwright.config.ts` and the existing `e2e/fidelity` spec shape as the loop leaves them, plus the per-class tolerance map in `e2e/fidelity/tolerance.ts`, which `BRIEF.md:49` makes authoritative (screens 0.0030, dialogs 0.0035, empty/loading/error 0.0020, full-page 0.0025; Main and Light exact at 0). **A test never reads a tolerance or a mask from `FIDELITY.md`** — that column is informational.

**Inputs.** A clone of the pushed `terum-skills-app`; its `FIDELITY.md` (read-only, for which boards are `locked` and therefore in scope); `e2e/fidelity/tolerance.ts`.

**Deliverables.** A per-platform baseline directory committed beside the Linux one, a `check` script variant that runs the platform's own Gate B, and one line in the repo README saying which platforms have baselines. **Every baseline is generated from the app, never from `.shots`.**

**Acceptance (owner alone).** On the owner's own machine: generate the baselines, then a **second** run green against them with zero updates; plus `npm run check` minus `e2e:fidelity` green (`typecheck`, `lint`, `vitest`, `e2e:routes`), which is exactly the command `TH:79` names.

**Must not touch.** `~/Projects/SSM/design/terum-skills/.shots/**` and the whole design folder; **Gate A, `FIDELITY.md`, `MASKS.md` and any lock** — those are Teddy's under §2.1 and a Mac render is not a valid Gate A oracle (`TH:61`); `src/screens/**`, `src/components/**`, `src/fixtures/**`, `tools/**`, `src/styles/tokens.css`. **Plus the standing rules:** nothing writes Teddy's four paths while any loop runs (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17`), and no printed string changes (`BRIEF.md:28`; `TH:61`). **If a Gate B baseline disagrees with a Gate A lock, that is a finding for Teddy, not a re-lock.**

**Blocking decisions.** None of Teddy's §6 rows. Its only gate is the push.

---

#### POST2 — Storybook over the nine specimen sheets

**Owner role:** frontend helper. **Size:** M. **Start:** after the loop ends **and** Teddy pushes. **Maps to nothing in `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` — additional.**

**Goal and why it matters.** `BRIEF.md:44` cuts Storybook **"tonight"**, and the reason is scheduling: it adds a dependency and a config the loop must carry while its only objective is 87 locked boards. Afterwards the case is the opposite way round — the nine sheets Gate A **excludes by definition** (`*States`, `*Hovers`, `*Panes`, `SkillCard`; `BRIEF.md:49`) are precisely the component-level surface no pixel gate will ever cover, so they are unprotected today and stay unprotected unless somebody builds this.

**Contract.** The Base UI wrappers and the three-mode header as the loop shipped them, plus the nine specimen sheets as the *reference drawings* — **read-only references, never a diff target**, since they are out of Gate A's scope on purpose.

**Acceptance (owner alone).** `storybook build` green in CI and one story per wrapper and per header mode, each rendering with no console error; **no story asserts a pixel count**.

**Must not touch.** The design folder; Gate A, `FIDELITY.md`, `MASKS.md`; `src/styles/tokens.css` and `src/fixtures/**` (generated by `tools/export-design.py` from `build.py`, and regenerating them is Teddy's). **Plus the standing rules** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17`; `BRIEF.md:28`). **Adding Storybook while any loop is running re-opens `BRIEF.md:44` — do not.**

---

#### POST3 — Accessibility: axe per route

**Owner role:** frontend helper. **Size:** S. **Start:** after the loop ends **and** Teddy pushes. **Maps to nothing in `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` — additional.**

**Goal and why it matters.** §7 withholds axe-per-route with the Base UI wrappers because it collides with M1 while M1 is being written. It is the one item in that row with **its own pass/fail that needs no shot and no design folder**, so once the files stop moving it separates cleanly — and it is checkable on any platform, which nothing else in the frontend is.

**Contract.** The route list `BRIEF.md:48` pins (HashRouter: `#/library/global`, `#/library/project/:name`, `#/skill/:ref` with its tab/dialog/rail states, `#/inbox`, `#/inbox/:id`, `#/marketplace` and its six sub-routes, `#/share`, `#/settings/:section` across eleven sections, `#/onboarding/:step` across six, and the reserved `#/search`) and the existing `e2e/routes` suite, which already asserts at least 30 route-plus-state smoke cases (`BRIEF.md:50`).

**Acceptance (owner alone).** An axe pass per route in `e2e/routes`, green, with every accepted violation carrying a written reason in the same file. Runs anywhere, no design folder, no `.shots`.

**Must not touch.** `src/screens/**` and `src/components/**` — **a violation is reported, not fixed here**, because fixing one is a component change and components are the loop's until the push and Teddy's design call afterwards where the drawn surface is implicated; the design folder; Gate A and its files. **Plus the standing rules** (`TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17`; `BRIEF.md:28`). **A contrast violation against a drawn colour is a design finding for Teddy, never a token edit** — `src/styles/tokens.css` is generated from `build.py`'s `TOKENS`.

---

## 8. Risks of this split, and the mitigation

| Risk | Why it is real here | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| **The contract drifts while both sides build.** SIDE2 codes against K1 while Ryan is still editing it; SIDE1 codes against K2 while the loop writes `types.ts` | K1 is *provably* incomplete today (no `main`, no `exports`, no `types`), and K2 does not exist as a file at all | **SIDE0 is the mitigation for K1** — a machine-checked name list that flips from `deep-import-only` to `bare` and fails loudly on a missing symbol. **SIDE1 is the mitigation for K2** — a conformance suite the loop's adapter must also pass, so drift is a red test rather than a discovery at M6. Both were designed for exactly this |
| **The CLI moves under everyone.** `b5c0507` is a pin, not a promise; sixteen commits landed between the investigation's pin and this one | Ten costing batches already carry stale `8bf12a8` line numbers with verified drift (`guard.ts:9→:10`, `cli.ts` ls `:87→:93`, `invite :82-86→:89-92`), and the pin's own `CHANGES-since-8bf12a8.txt` lists the sixteen commits that produced those drifts. (An earlier draft quoted `HO:58` here for "origin/main moved three times during this session". **That line does not exist** — `handoff.md` is 57 lines, and the sentence appears nowhere in it. The claim is retired as NOT FOUND; what `HO:42` does record is the state actually observed: "`~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` on `main` @ `b5c0507`, clean except the untracked `.planning/` docs". The row's substance stands on the sixteen commits and the three verified drifts, neither of which needs the quotation) | Every package names its inputs as `<path>:<line> @ b5c0507` and every spec delta **re-derives** them before use. Ryan's own repo is the authority; a citation is a hypothesis until re-read. This document's judges found five off-by-one citations in its own drafts — the corrections are in §8.1 |
| **The Mac owner is unnamed and the Mac's architecture is unverified.** Every Mac package is contingent on an individual nobody has named and a machine nobody has described | `TB:199` and `HO:48` predate `TEAM-HANDOFF.md`, which supersedes them on the existence question: `TH:12`, `:13`, `:43` assign the Mac work to "Mac teammates" on "their Macs", and `TH:61` is a not-today list written for people who already have it. What survives is the **arch** (`TB:244`, and `TH:49` makes recording it a deliverable) and the individual's name | Decision 3, re-scoped: **which teammate, and Apple Silicon or Intel.** MAC1 is written without a name rather than unassigned, and **no `TH:45-59` item is cancelled by that**. CI1 still delivers value with nobody to open the artifact — a green run and a downloadable Release are the proof option E needs. The app still boots in a browser on mock data, which is the stated objective |
| **The backend developer and the CI owner are unnamed.** Six packages — **SIDE0, SIDE1, SIDE2, SIDE3, CI1, CI2** — are written against two roles no individual has been named for, and **SIDE2 is §5.1 link 3, the critical path** | Only **Ryan** and **Ajay** are named **by name** anywhere in this plan. `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:13` does place "M6 groundwork: the sidecar that drives the real CLI" with the Mac teammates "by Teddy's objective", so the work has an assigned group; what it lacks is a person per lane. An earlier draft went the other way and treated the roles' *existence* as unverified, which cancelled six of `TH`'s seven day-one items by implication | **Decision 19, re-scoped: which of the named teammates holds each lane.** All six are written without a name rather than assigned to one nobody has supplied, and **none is withdrawn from `TH`**. §0 now counts under `TH`'s own assignment. The mitigation that costs nothing: the **CI-owner role touches no CLI-repo file**, so Ryan can double as it (CI1/CI2 write a scratch repo and the app repo, never his). The backend-dev role is a full second lane and has no such cheap fallback — **if it goes unstaffed, M6 has no owner and the plan should say so rather than schedule it** |
| **A blank window makes MAC1 yield nothing.** `tauri#15517` is open against exactly the 2.11.x line this experiment pinned, and `macos-latest` is macOS 26 | `TB:245` item 5 | The package instructs: report the macOS version and **stop**. A blank window is a finding, not a failure of the sitting. Budget one sitting and expect one round trip |
| **Option E's iteration cost is unbounded and unmeasured.** Each chrome fix is a rebuild the Mac owner re-downloads, possibly re-walking the Gatekeeper ritual | `TB:243` item 3 records three unknowns that recur per rebuild | Decision 2 carries the threshold explicitly: **more than about three iterations and B was cheaper.** Measure the first two |
| **What can be verified from this box has a hard limit.** Nothing was run on macOS; **no workflow was run for this app in the session that produced `TB`** (`TB:131`); **`externalBin` and any `invoke` round trip were never exercised *on this box*** (`TB:249` item 9); `cargo check` skips code generation (`TB:250` item 10). *Each phrased at the source's own scope — earlier drafts widened two of them to "in any session" and "anywhere", which is a stronger claim than any source makes* | The standing rule at the top of `TB` says so in as many words | Three of the thirteen packages exist *to convert an unknown into a measurement* — CI1, SIDE3, MAC1 — and each states what it cannot prove. No package's acceptance depends on a claim this box made about another platform |
| **The gap-costing doc's change lists live in a second document, so the two can drift.** The risk has changed shape rather than gone away: `GC` **exists** now, so the old risk — "a handoff that says 'see the gap-costing doc' points teammates at nothing" — is closed, and duplication replaces it | `GC` is 949 KB and carries all 196 rows with their change lists; this document is a thousand lines of package structure. A row written out in both drifts the first time either is corrected — which is exactly what happened to CLI2's four bare labels while `GC` was still pending | **One home per row: `GC` §2 for CLI-side change lists, §3 for the app and design halves, §4 for MEDIUM and HARD, §1 for verdicts and approvers, §6 for the design consequences by board.** This document cites **section plus row id**, and restates a change list only where an owner would otherwise be blocked (RM-S1 and the §5.2 `personSchema` constraint in CLI2, both flagged as such in the text). **The direction of authority is fixed: where a verdict here and a verdict in `GC` disagree, `GC`'s reconciled row wins** — it is the one that went through two refuters. The 8/135/53 split this row used to point at is superseded by `GC`'s own 196-costed / 0-uncosted count; §7's row says so |
| **Two documents describe the same day, and a reader could take the wrong one as the list.** `TH` §3 assigns seven items to "Mac teammates"; §4 here portions thirteen packages across five owner roles (§5.2's sixth lane is Teddy's own) | `TH` assumes a Mac owner exists, routes CLI work through people who do not own the CLI repo, and gives one role five unrelated jobs — while this document applies a NOT VERIFIED standard to three roles and re-scopes four of the seven items to a different owner role | **Resolved by ordering, not by deletion: `TH` is the list Teddy has already handed out and it stands; this document is the work behind it.** §0.1 maps every package to the `TH` item it implements — with the re-scope stated in the same cell — or labels it additional, and §8.1 is the same map from the `TH` side. **Do not delete or rewrite `TH`**: it carries the standing write rule (`:17`), the loop-start recipe (`:19-41`), the not-today list (`:61`) and the end-of-run sequence (`:73-79`), none of which is reproduced here in full. Where a re-scope changes who does a `TH` item, the change is Teddy's to accept — it is offered in §0.1, not applied |

It now sits on the item-5 row above, which is what §0.1 says.)* |

## 9. Evidence

**Pins.** CLI archive `scratchpad/costing/cli-pin`, `.PIN` = `b5c0507` (full `b5c05079ccdfb1e40beccd764cdca4f8265a9e38`), with `CHANGES-since-8bf12a8.txt` listing the 16 commits (share→connect rename; HYG6 as a warning; IE4 key-free CI + `receiptCheck`; setup multi-connect; launch-aware hints). **The pin still holds:** `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills` is at `b5c0507`, working tree clean except untracked `.planning/` files — which now include this document and `GC`, both of which goal constraint (a) permits and neither of which may be committed into that checkout. Design snapshot `scratchpad/costing/design-snapshot`, `build.py` md5 `43f4d0901f4eb618dc33f692f70a3aa2`, 4,526 lines, `canvas.json` 8 pages / 98 artboards, 100 `.shots/*.png` — **frozen, and §1 records the measured drift to the live file (md5 `e98f519e…`, 4,658 lines, 99 artboards, 99 shots) plus the fourteen anchor line-shifts, so every bare `build.py:<line>` here is re-grepped by content rather than trusted.**

**Measured for the 2026-09-08 revision, read-only, without writing to any of the four locked paths.** `git -C ~/Projects/terum-skills-app log --oneline` (**29 commits, 3 Codex merges at 2026-09-08T04:26Z** — 28 eight minutes earlier; this number moves and must be re-read, never quoted from here), `git -C ~/Projects/terum-skills-app remote -v` (empty), `FIDELITY.md` (**87 rows, 27 `locked` at the same instant**, re-read with `grep -cE '\| *locked *\|'`), `GAPS.md` (37 `- ` lines under three headings, `:7-24` / `:28-32` / `:36-49` — stable across the run so far), `src/backend/{Backend.ts,types.ts,mock/index.ts}` for the seam names and the five capability flags, `.planning/specs/` and `.planning/codex-runs/`, `git -C ~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills status` for the pin, and the live `build.py` / `canvas.json` md5s and line anchors. **`GC` is on disk** at `/home/teniroo/Projects/SSM/terum-skills/.planning/research/2026-09-07-desktop-app-gap-costing.md` (949 KB) and is this document's change-list authority; its own evidence section carries the batch files.

**The three partitions this plan was distilled from**, all under `scratchpad/handoff/`:
- `split-by-owner.md` — **the winner in all three judge passes.** Eleven packages by owner role; the structure, the package bodies, the kept list and the stale-facts table below are largely its work.
- `split-by-contract.md` — fifteen packages, one per side of one contract. **Grafted:** the eight-contract front table (§3), the import-surface contract test (SIDE0), the `Result.ok === false` three-meanings rule and the `pending`-before-placement fact (SIDE2), decision 5 (sidecar repo), decision 6 (the C1-C17 tolerance), and the corrected `ICON_PATHS` / `TOKENS` / `check_*` counts.
- `split-by-critical-path.md` — packages by wave and by risk. **Grafted:** the critical-path chain and the four-verdicts table (§5), pulling SIDE3 into week 1 against CI1's bundle, the lane table's file-disjointness assertion, CI2 (production release + `HANDOFF-M5.md`), the `src/backend/tauri/**` file assignment for SIDE2, the "no contract exists" exclusion row (§7), and the package-internal-decisions list (§6.1).

**Judge notes.** Three adversarial passes over the three partitions, each returning a winner, grafts and defects. All three chose `split-by-owner`. Roughly twenty defects were raised; each was re-verified against the pin before being accepted or rejected. Accepted and fixed here: the `process.exit`-vs-`process.exitCode` mechanism (CLI1), the ambiguous `readme.ts` (CLI2/CLI3/SIDE2) — **and `readme.test.ts`, which is ambiguous in exactly the same way and was left bare in CLI3's acceptance line until this revision; both are now full-path everywhere**, the nine-vs-eight test count (CLI3), `INV:197`→`:195` and FE1's owner role, the `TB:235`→`:233` and `TB:203`→`:199` anchors, `HO:13`→`:11` and `HO:47`→`:48`, `ls.ts:18`→`:20`, `INV:307`→`:305`, `INV:129-134`→`:127-132`, the closed 1420-vs-5173 port question, the "45+9" icon count, CLI4's three-gates-in-one-package (split; `per_case` retired), CI1's unassigned production release (now CI2), SIDE2's ownerless `src/backend/tauri/`, and the missing "no contract exists" exclusion row. Rejected: none of substance; the judges' defect lists were mutually consistent.

**Reader reports** — **session-scratch provenance, not a teammate input.** They lived under this session's `scratchpad/handoff/reports/` and are gone with it; every fact any package took from them is inlined in that package (the 20 `run` signatures are reproduced in **CLI1**).
